x= 218.5 y=  99.2 x1= 378.8 sz=[15.9] ['URWPalladioL-Bold'] | DeepSeek-V4.1-Flash:
x= 130.3 y= 119.1 x1= 465.0 sz=[15.9] ['URWPalladioL-Bold'] | Pushing the Limits of KV Cache Compression
x= 265.7 y= 141.8 x1= 329.5 sz=[11.0] ['URWPalladioL-Roma'] | DeepSeek-AI
x= 236.0 y= 155.9 x1= 356.8 sz=[11.0] ['LMMonoLt10-Bold'] | research@deepseek.com
x= 269.0 y= 177.8 x1= 326.3 sz=[14.9] ['URWPalladioL-Bold'] | Abstract
x=  70.5 y= 201.4 x1= 526.2 sz=[10.8] ['URWPalladioL-Roma'] | The widespread adoption of long-horizon agents has made model workloads increasingly input-
x=  70.9 y= 215.5 x1= 525.8 sz=[11.1] ['URWPalladioL-Roma'] | heavy. Although prior work has substantially reduced the cost of long-context computation,
x=  70.5 y= 229.9 x1= 524.4 sz=[11.1] ['URWPalladioL-Roma'] | prefill remains computationally expensive, and large KV caches continue to strain HBM and
x=  70.9 y= 244.3 x1= 524.4 sz=[11.0] ['URWPalladioL-Roma'] | SSD capacity and data-transfer bandwidth. Together, these compute, storage, and bandwidth
x=  70.9 y= 258.6 x1= 524.4 sz=[11.0] ['URWPalladioL-Roma'] | demands constitute the primary bottleneck to further lowering deployment costs. To address
x=  70.9 y= 272.9 x1= 525.5 sz=[11.1] ['URWPalladioL-Roma'] | this challenge, we introduce DeepSeek-V4.1-Flash, a multimodal Mixture-of-Experts (MoE)
x=  70.9 y= 287.3 x1= 526.3 sz=[11.1] ['URWPalladioL-Roma'] | model with 552B backbone parameters and support for contexts of up to one million tokens.
x=  70.3 y= 301.6 x1= 524.4 sz=[11.1] ['URWPalladioL-Roma'] | With its Causal Encoder-Decoder (CED) architecture, the model activates 16B parameters
x=  70.5 y= 316.0 x1= 524.4 sz=[11.1] ['URWPalladioL-Roma'] | per token during decode but only 8B parameters during prefill, substantially improving cost
x=  70.9 y= 330.4 x1= 526.2 sz=[11.0] ['URWPalladioL-Roma'] | efficiency for agentic workloads. To push the limits of KV cache compression, DeepSeek-V4.1-
x=  70.9 y= 344.8 x1= 524.4 sz=[10.9] ['URWPalladioL-Roma'] | Flash combines cross-layer KV cache reuse in Compressed Sparse Attention 2 (CSA2) with FP4
x=  70.9 y= 359.1 x1= 524.4 sz=[11.0] ['URWPalladioL-Roma'] | KV caching. These designs reduce its global KV cache footprint (always in HBM) to 890 bytes
x=  70.5 y= 373.5 x1= 524.4 sz=[10.9] ['URWPalladioL-Roma'] | per token, roughly 1/4 of the corresponding footprint of DeepSeek-V4-Flash. Further, through
x=  70.9 y= 387.7 x1= 524.4 sz=[11.0] ['URWPalladioL-Roma'] | a dedicated deployment optimization known as SWA Bounded Replay, DeepSeek-V4.1-Flash
x=  70.9 y= 402.2 x1= 524.4 sz=[10.9] ['URWPalladioL-Roma'] | reduces its persistent KV cache footprint (always on SSD or in host memory) to roughly 1/8 of
x=  70.9 y= 416.4 x1= 524.4 sz=[11.1] ['URWPalladioL-Roma'] | that of DeepSeek-V4-Flash. Despite its much smaller KV cache footprint, the model delivers
x=  70.9 y= 430.9 x1= 524.4 sz=[10.9] ['URWPalladioL-Roma'] | substantially better performance than the baseline. In addition, we streamline the DeepSeek-V4
x=  70.9 y= 445.2 x1= 526.2 sz=[10.8] ['URWPalladioL-Roma'] | architecture and introduce several efficient architectural extensions. We pretrain DeepSeek-V4.1-
x=  70.9 y= 459.6 x1= 525.8 sz=[10.8] ['URWPalladioL-Roma'] | Flash on a multimodal corpus comprising 45T tokens and conduct comprehensive post-training,
x=  70.6 y= 473.9 x1= 524.4 sz=[10.8] ['URWPalladioL-Roma'] | yielding strong performance across diverse text-based and multimodal agentic scenarios. Model
x=  70.9 y= 488.2 x1= 497.5 sz=[11.0] ['URWPalladioL-Roma'] | checkpoints are available at https://huggingface.co/deepseek-ai/DeepSeek-V4.1-Flash.

x=  70.9 y=  86.6 x1= 161.9 sz=[13.0] ['URWPalladioL-Bold'] | 1. Introduction
x=  70.4 y= 113.5 x1= 526.2 sz=[10.9] ['URWPalladioL-Roma'] | Applications of long-horizon agents have expanded rapidly in recent years, making ultra-long-
x=  70.9 y= 127.0 x1= 524.4 sz=[11.0] ['URWPalladioL-Roma'] | context processing an increasingly important model workload. Supporting such workloads
x=  70.9 y= 140.5 x1= 525.8 sz=[11.0] ['URWPalladioL-Roma'] | requires not only efficient long-sequence processing, but also the persistent storage, reuse,
x=  70.9 y= 154.0 x1= 524.4 sz=[11.0] ['URWPalladioL-Roma'] | and transfer of large KV caches. KV cache management has therefore become a foundational
x=  70.9 y= 167.7 x1= 525.8 sz=[10.9] ['URWPalladioL-Roma'] | capability for model deployment, while introducing substantial challenges across computation,
x=  70.9 y= 181.3 x1= 524.4 sz=[10.8] ['URWPalladioL-Roma'] | storage, and communication. Prior advances in sparse attention (DeepSeek-AI, 2025, 2026b) have
x=  70.9 y= 194.7 x1= 524.4 sz=[11.0] ['URWPalladioL-Roma'] | significantly reduced the computational cost of long-sequence processing, making persistent
x=  70.9 y= 208.3 x1= 380.6 sz=[10.9] ['URWPalladioL-Roma'] | storage and data movement increasingly prominent bottlenecks.
x=  87.8 y= 228.7 x1= 524.4 sz=[10.8] ['URWPalladioL-Roma'] | Specifically, DeepSeek-V4 (DeepSeek-AI, 2026b) combines a global attention branch spanning
x=  70.9 y= 242.3 x1= 524.4 sz=[10.8] ['URWPalladioL-Roma'] | the full context with local Sliding-Window Attention (SWA). The global branch maintains global
x=  70.9 y= 255.7 x1= 524.4 sz=[11.0] ['URWPalladioL-Roma'] | KV, comprising main KV and indexer K, while SWA maintains local KV states. For a fixed
x=  70.4 y= 269.2 x1= 524.8 sz=[11.0] ['URWPalladioL-Roma'] | window size, SWA KV storage is bounded independently of sequence length. For sufficiently
x=  70.9 y= 282.8 x1= 524.4 sz=[10.9] ['URWPalladioL-Bold', 'URWPalladioL-Roma'] | long sequences, global KV therefore dominates the runtime KV footprint, which is constrained
x=  70.9 y= 296.4 x1= 524.4 sz=[10.8] ['URWPalladioL-Bold', 'URWPalladioL-Roma'] | by HBM capacity. In addition, certain KV are persisted for prefix reuse, referred to as persistent
x=  70.9 y= 309.8 x1= 524.4 sz=[11.0] ['URWPalladioL-Bold', 'URWPalladioL-Roma'] | KV caches, which are constrained by SSD and host memory capacity. I/O and interconnect
x=  70.9 y= 323.4 x1= 524.4 sz=[11.0] ['URWPalladioL-Roma'] | bandwidth also limit cache migration and loading. Together, these constraints limit serving
x=  70.9 y= 337.1 x1= 524.4 sz=[10.9] ['URWPalladioL-Roma'] | throughput, increase deployment costs, and ultimately hinder the deployment and adoption of
x=  70.9 y= 350.6 x1= 428.7 sz=[10.9] ['URWPalladioL-Roma'] | agents over longer task horizons and across broader application scenarios.
x=  87.8 y= 370.8 x1= 524.4 sz=[11.0] ['URWPalladioL-Roma'] | Further reducing the KV cache footprint is therefore critical to alleviating storage and
x=  70.9 y= 384.4 x1= 524.4 sz=[11.0] ['URWPalladioL-Roma'] | communication bottlenecks and lowering the cost of long-context serving. To this end, we
x=  70.9 y= 397.9 x1= 524.6 sz=[11.0] ['URWPalladioL-Roma'] | develop DeepSeek-V4.1-Flash, a multimodal Mixture-of-Experts (MoE) model designed for
x=  70.9 y= 411.5 x1= 525.8 sz=[10.9] ['URWPalladioL-Roma'] | more aggressive KV cache compression. DeepSeek-V4.1-Flash has 552B backbone parameters,
x=  70.9 y= 425.1 x1= 526.3 sz=[10.9] ['URWPalladioL-Roma'] | natively supports multimodal inputs, and accommodates contexts of up to one million tokens.
x=  70.3 y= 438.7 x1= 524.4 sz=[10.8] ['URWPalladioL-Roma'] | We adopt a Causal Encoder-Decoder (CED) architecture, in which decoder global KV is projected
x=  70.9 y= 452.3 x1= 524.6 sz=[10.8] ['URWPalladioL-Roma'] | from the final encoder hidden states. This design enables the model to activate 8B parameters per
x=  70.9 y= 465.8 x1= 524.8 sz=[10.8] ['URWPalladioL-Roma'] | token during prefill and 16B during decode, which is particularly cost-effective for input-heavy
x=  70.9 y= 479.3 x1= 526.2 sz=[10.9] ['URWPalladioL-Roma'] | agentic scenarios. Despite being considerably larger than DeepSeek-V4-Flash, DeepSeek-V4.1-
x=  70.9 y= 492.8 x1= 524.4 sz=[11.0] ['URWPalladioL-Roma'] | Flash requires only approximately 1/4 as much runtime KV cache storage and 1/8 as much
x=  70.5 y= 506.3 x1= 524.4 sz=[11.0] ['URWPalladioL-Roma'] | persistent KV cache storage at the same sequence length. Moreover, DeepSeek-V4.1-Flash
x=  70.9 y= 520.0 x1= 364.3 sz=[10.9] ['URWPalladioL-Roma'] | delivers better overall performance than DeepSeek-V4-Flash.
x=  87.8 y= 540.3 x1= 526.2 sz=[10.9] ['URWPalladioL-Roma'] | This level of KV cache compression is achieved through joint optimizations in model archi-
x=  70.9 y= 553.8 x1= 524.4 sz=[11.0] ['URWPalladioL-Roma'] | tecture, cache precision, and deployment strategy. Conceptually, DeepSeek-V4 can be viewed
x=  70.9 y= 567.4 x1= 524.4 sz=[10.9] ['URWPalladioL-Roma'] | as an SWA-based local-processing backbone augmented with compressed global context. This
x=  70.5 y= 580.8 x1= 524.4 sz=[11.0] ['URWPalladioL-Roma'] | perspective motivates us to focus on simplifying the global branch while largely preserving
x=  70.9 y= 594.5 x1= 524.4 sz=[10.9] ['URWPalladioL-Roma'] | the local attention design. At the architectural level, we design Compressed Sparse Attention 2
x=  70.5 y= 608.0 x1= 524.4 sz=[11.0] ['URWPalladioL-Roma'] | (CSA2), which applies cross-layer reuse to global KV (including main KV and indexer K) and
x=  70.5 y= 621.5 x1= 524.4 sz=[11.0] ['URWPalladioL-Roma'] | Top-K indices to substantially reduce KV cache storage. CSA2 has three statically assigned
x=  70.9 y= 635.0 x1= 526.3 sz=[11.0] ['URWPalladioL-Roma'] | modes: Full, Reindex, and Reuse. Full Mode generates global KV and performs indexing.
x=  70.9 y= 648.6 x1= 524.4 sz=[11.0] ['URWPalladioL-Roma'] | Reindex Mode reuses the global KV from a preceding layer, and uses its own indexer Q to
x=  70.9 y= 662.1 x1= 524.4 sz=[11.0] ['URWPalladioL-Roma'] | rescore the shared indexer K and select fresh Top-K indices. Reuse Mode reuses both global
x=  70.9 y= 675.7 x1= 524.4 sz=[11.0] ['URWPalladioL-Roma'] | KV and the Top-K indices in a preceding layer, and directly performs sparse attention. In all
x=  70.9 y= 689.4 x1= 524.8 sz=[10.8] ['URWPalladioL-Roma'] | three modes, each layer retains its own global Q and SWA KV. Sharing global KV and indexer K
x=  70.9 y= 702.8 x1= 524.4 sz=[11.0] ['URWPalladioL-Roma'] | reduces duplicated cache storage. In addition, different from DeepSeek-V4 that employs the
x=  70.9 y= 716.5 x1= 525.8 sz=[10.8] ['URWPalladioL-Roma'] | Compressed Sparse Attention (CSA)–Heavily Compressed Attention (HCA) hybrid architecture,
x=  70.9 y= 730.0 x1= 524.4 sz=[10.8] ['URWPalladioL-Roma'] | DeepSeek-V4.1-Flash uses pure CSA2. At the cache-precision level, we use FP4 global KV caches
x=  70.9 y= 743.4 x1= 524.8 sz=[11.0] ['URWPalladioL-Roma'] | during training with only marginal performance degradation. Together, CSA2 and FP4 KV
x= 294.9 y= 778.9 x1= 300.4 sz=[10.9] ['URWPalladioL-Roma'] | 4

x=  70.9 y= 240.2 x1= 524.7 sz=[10.9, 11.0] ['URWPalladioL-Roma', 'txsys'] | Figure 2 | Single-token Decode FLOPs versus context length across generations of DeepSeek
x=  70.9 y= 255.0 x1= 525.8 sz=[10.8] ['URWPalladioL-Roma'] | models. We account for compute precision by weighting BF16, FP8, and FP4 operations by 1, 0.5,
x=  70.9 y= 268.5 x1= 524.4 sz=[10.8] ['URWPalladioL-Roma'] | and 0.25, respectively. DeepSeek-V4.1-Flash maintains nearly constant Decode FLOPs as context

x=  70.9 y= 317.0 x1= 524.4 sz=[10.9] ['URWPalladioL-Roma'] | caching reduce global KV cache storage to approximately 1/4 of that of DeepSeek-V4-Flash, as
x=  70.9 y= 330.4 x1= 524.4 sz=[11.0] ['URWPalladioL-Roma'] | shown in Figure 1(b). At the deployment level, DeepSeek-V4.1-Flash, like DeepSeek-V4, uses
x=  70.9 y= 344.0 x1= 524.8 sz=[11.0] ['URWPalladioL-Roma'] | Sliding-Window Attention (SWA) in every layer. In DeepSeek-V4, we use a hybrid strategy
x=  70.9 y= 357.5 x1= 524.6 sz=[11.0] ['URWPalladioL-Roma'] | to balance the storage cost of persisting SWA KV caches against the computation required for
x=  70.9 y= 369.0 x1= 525.8 sz=[8.0, 10.0, 10.9, 11.0] ['URWPalladioL-Roma', 'XCharterMathMI', 'txsys'] | exact reconstruction. Exact reconstruction requires replaying the most recent 𝐿× 𝑛win tokens,
x=  70.4 y= 382.6 x1= 525.8 sz=[8.0, 10.0, 11.0] ['URWPalladioL-Roma', 'XCharterMathMI'] | where 𝐿is the number of layers and 𝑛win is the SWA window size. In DeepSeek-V4.1-Flash,
x=  70.4 y= 398.2 x1= 524.8 sz=[11.0] ['URWPalladioL-Roma'] | we introduce SWA Bounded Replay, which approximately reconstructs the required SWA KV
x=  70.9 y= 409.7 x1= 524.4 sz=[8.0, 10.0, 11.0] ['URWPalladioL-Roma', 'XCharterMathMI'] | states by replaying only the most recent 𝑛win tokens. Our experiments show that this incurs
x=  70.9 y= 425.3 x1= 524.4 sz=[10.9] ['URWPalladioL-Roma'] | only negligible performance degradation. This finding establishes a new storage–computation
x=  70.9 y= 439.0 x1= 524.4 sz=[10.8] ['URWPalladioL-Roma'] | trade-off, allowing us to avoid persisting SWA KV cache to SSD while incurring a small amount
x=  70.9 y= 452.5 x1= 524.6 sz=[10.8] ['URWPalladioL-Roma'] | of prefill recomputation. With SWA Bounded Replay, the persistent KV cache footprint is further
x=  70.9 y= 465.9 x1= 524.4 sz=[11.0] ['URWPalladioL-Roma'] | reduced to approximately 1/8 of that of DeepSeek-V4-Flash. Together, these optimizations
x=  70.9 y= 479.6 x1= 524.6 sz=[10.8] ['URWPalladioL-Roma'] | greatly ease pressure on HBM and SSD capacity, reduce deployment costs, and pave the way for
x=  70.9 y= 493.1 x1= 209.0 sz=[10.9] ['URWPalladioL-Roma'] | deployment at a larger scale.
x=  87.8 y= 513.5 x1= 526.3 sz=[10.8] ['URWPalladioL-Roma'] | Complementing CED and CSA2, we further streamline the original DeepSeek-V4 architecture.
x=  70.4 y= 526.8 x1= 524.4 sz=[10.8] ['URWPalladioL-Ital', 'URWPalladioL-Roma'] | Additionally, we upgrade the original mHC (Xie et al., 2026) design to Single-Pass mHC, with an
x=  70.9 y= 540.3 x1= 524.4 sz=[10.9] ['URWPalladioL-Ital', 'URWPalladioL-Roma'] | accompanying Mega-mHC deployment kernel that halves activation memory traffic relative to
x=  70.9 y= 554.0 x1= 525.8 sz=[11.0] ['URWPalladioL-Roma'] | the original four-kernel implementation. Furthermore, we integrate the Engram (Cheng et al.,
x=  70.9 y= 567.5 x1= 524.4 sz=[11.0] ['URWPalladioL-Roma'] | 2026b) conditional memory module to strengthen model capabilities. We also introduce the
x=  70.9 y= 581.1 x1= 524.8 sz=[10.9] ['URWPalladioL-Roma'] | DSpark (Cheng et al., 2026a) speculative decoding architecture to improve decoding efficiency
x=  70.9 y= 594.7 x1= 524.4 sz=[11.0] ['URWPalladioL-Roma'] | through semi-autoregressive draft generation and confidence-scheduled verification. With all
x=  70.9 y= 608.2 x1= 526.2 sz=[11.0] ['URWPalladioL-Roma'] | the architectural improvements combined, the single-token Decode FLOPs of DeepSeek-V4.1-
x=  70.9 y= 621.8 x1= 524.4 sz=[10.9] ['URWPalladioL-Roma'] | Flash remain nearly constant across context lengths. Figure 2 shows that extending the context
x=  70.9 y= 635.4 x1= 524.4 sz=[10.9] ['URWPalladioL-Roma'] | length 256-fold, from 4K to 1M, increases its Decode FLOPs by only 1/4, significantly less than
x=  70.9 y= 648.9 x1= 286.8 sz=[10.9] ['URWPalladioL-Roma'] | the growth observed for DeepSeek-V4-Flash.
x=  87.8 y= 669.3 x1= 524.6 sz=[10.8] ['URWPalladioL-Roma'] | To fully realize the KV cache compression benefits of these architectural designs and further
x=  70.9 y= 682.8 x1= 526.2 sz=[10.8] ['URWPalladioL-Roma'] | improve training and inference efficiency, we systematically co-optimize the training infrastruc-
x=  70.9 y= 696.3 x1= 524.4 sz=[10.9] ['URWPalladioL-Roma'] | ture and inference system for DeepSeek-V4.1-Flash, ensuring efficient and scalable large-scale
x=  70.9 y= 709.8 x1= 526.2 sz=[10.9] ['URWPalladioL-Roma'] | multimodal training and long-context deployment. Training infrastructure supports disaggre-
x=  70.9 y= 723.4 x1= 524.4 sz=[11.0] ['URWPalladioL-Roma'] | gated vision-encoder execution, balanced image sharding for long sequences, and cross-stage
x=  70.9 y= 737.0 x1= 526.2 sz=[10.8] ['URWPalladioL-Roma'] | shared-state management for attention reuse. The inference system implements Encoder and De-
x= 294.9 y= 778.9 x1= 300.4 sz=[10.9] ['URWPalladioL-Roma'] | 5

========== PAGE 6 (draw=0) ==========
x=  70.9 y=  88.3 x1= 524.4 sz=[10.8] ['URWPalladioL-Roma'] | coder SWA Bounded Replay paths. Further optimizations include communication–computation
x=  70.9 y= 101.6 x1= 524.4 sz=[11.0] ['URWPalladioL-Roma'] | overlap, sharded Engram embedding tables, and inference kernel fusion. In particular, each
x=  70.9 y= 115.3 x1= 524.4 sz=[10.9] ['URWPalladioL-Roma'] | CSA2 Reuse Mode layer executes with only 15 kernels during prefill and 11 during decode. We
x=  70.9 y= 128.8 x1= 525.8 sz=[10.9] ['URWPalladioL-Roma'] | also separate long-lived global KV storage from short-lived encoder SWA KV in host memory,
x=  70.9 y= 142.4 x1= 460.3 sz=[10.9] ['URWPalladioL-Roma'] | using bounded replay to approximately reconstruct missing encoder SWA states.
x=  87.8 y= 162.6 x1= 524.4 sz=[11.0] ['URWPalladioL-Roma'] | During pre-training, we train DeepSeek-V4.1-Flash on a large-scale multimodal corpus
x=  70.9 y= 176.2 x1= 525.8 sz=[11.0] ['URWPalladioL-Roma'] | comprising 45T tokens. Sparse attention is trained from scratch at a sequence length of 64K,
x=  70.4 y= 189.7 x1= 524.4 sz=[11.0] ['URWPalladioL-Roma'] | without any dense attention warmup stages. After pre-training, the model possesses native
x=  70.9 y= 203.3 x1= 525.8 sz=[10.9] ['URWPalladioL-Roma'] | multimodal capabilities and supports contexts of up to one million tokens. In our evaluations,
x=  70.9 y= 216.8 x1= 526.2 sz=[11.0] ['URWPalladioL-Roma'] | DeepSeek-V4.1-Flash-Base achieves world knowledge, reasoning and coding abilities compa-
x=  70.9 y= 230.4 x1= 525.8 sz=[11.0] ['URWPalladioL-Roma'] | rable to DeepSeek-V4-Pro-Base, and delivers 5%–10% improvements on held-out evaluations,
x=  70.9 y= 244.0 x1= 524.4 sz=[10.9] ['URWPalladioL-Roma'] | using only 1/3 total parameters and 1/4 activated parameters. Together, these results highlight
x=  70.9 y= 257.6 x1= 524.4 sz=[10.9] ['URWPalladioL-Roma'] | its strong parameter efficiency and reflect improvements in training data quality for real-world
x=  70.9 y= 271.1 x1= 132.0 sz=[10.9] ['URWPalladioL-Roma'] | deployment.
x=  87.8 y= 291.3 x1= 524.4 sz=[11.0] ['URWPalladioL-Roma'] | Building on this base model, we conduct post-training to elicit its reasoning and agentic
x=  70.9 y= 304.9 x1= 524.4 sz=[11.0] ['URWPalladioL-Roma'] | capabilities. In contrast to the architectural innovations described above, our post-training
x=  70.9 y= 318.5 x1= 524.4 sz=[10.9] ['URWPalladioL-Roma'] | introduces no algorithmic innovation: the recipe follows the standard paradigm of supervised
x=  70.9 y= 332.0 x1= 525.8 sz=[11.0] ['URWPalladioL-Roma'] | fine-tuning (SFT) followed by reinforcement learning (RL) and on-policy distillation (OPD),
x=  70.4 y= 345.6 x1= 524.4 sz=[10.9] ['URWPalladioL-Roma'] | without any modification beyond well-established practice used in DeepSeek-V4 development
x=  70.5 y= 359.1 x1= 524.7 sz=[11.0] ['URWPalladioL-Roma'] | (DeepSeek-AI, 2026b). All substantive changes lie instead in the data pipeline. We develop
x=  70.9 y= 372.7 x1= 526.2 sz=[10.9] ['URWPalladioL-Roma'] | large-scale automated pipelines for data synthesis and environment construction, and progres-
x=  70.9 y= 386.2 x1= 524.4 sz=[11.0] ['URWPalladioL-Roma'] | sively scale the data, tasks, and rollouts employed during RL, thereby extending the model’s
x=  70.9 y= 399.9 x1= 526.2 sz=[10.8] ['URWPalladioL-Roma'] | capabilities across textual, multimodal, and agentic domains. Figure 1(a) summarizes DeepSeek-

x=  70.9 y= 329.3 x1= 524.4 sz=[10.9] ['URWPalladioL-Bold', 'URWPalladioL-Roma', 'txsys'] | Figure 3 | Overall architecture of DeepSeek-V4.1-Flash. The 40-layer network is divided into

x=  70.9 y= 398.2 x1= 144.5 sz=[10.9] ['URWPalladioL-Roma'] | Sparse Indexer.
x=  70.9 y= 433.1 x1= 524.4 sz=[10.9] ['URWPalladioL-Roma'] | broad population of users. In summary, DeepSeek-V4.1-Flash simultaneously improves model
x=  70.9 y= 446.6 x1= 524.4 sz=[11.0] ['URWPalladioL-Roma'] | intelligence and inference efficiency while reducing deployment costs. It substantially lowers

x=  70.5 y= 487.2 x1= 524.4 sz=[11.0] ['URWPalladioL-Roma'] | point for our continued scaling efforts. Building on this foundation, we will pursue the joint
x=  70.9 y= 500.8 x1= 524.4 sz=[11.0] ['URWPalladioL-Roma'] | scaling of model architecture, pre-training, and post-training to further explore the frontier of
x=  70.9 y= 514.4 x1= 162.5 sz=[10.9] ['URWPalladioL-Roma'] | model intelligence.
x=  70.9 y= 548.9 x1= 160.4 sz=[13.0] ['URWPalladioL-Bold'] | 2. Architecture
x=  70.9 y= 576.8 x1= 141.8 sz=[10.9] ['URWPalladioL-Bold'] | 2.1. Overview
x=  70.9 y= 599.2 x1= 524.4 sz=[10.9] ['URWPalladioL-Roma'] | DeepSeek-V4.1-Flash is a multimodal mixture-of-experts (MoE) Transformer that takes images
x=  70.9 y= 612.8 x1= 524.4 sz=[10.8] ['URWPalladioL-Roma'] | and text as input and generates text autoregressively. Its language backbone comprises 40 causal
x=  70.5 y= 626.2 x1= 526.3 sz=[11.0] ['URWPalladioL-Roma'] | Transformer layers, organized into a 20-layer causal encoder followed by a 20-layer decoder.
x=  70.9 y= 639.8 x1= 524.6 sz=[11.0] ['URWPalladioL-Roma'] | Each layer incorporates both global attention and sliding window attention (SWA), except for
x=  70.9 y= 653.5 x1= 524.4 sz=[10.8] ['URWPalladioL-Roma'] | the first two layers, which use SWA only. A vision encoder and an MLP projector convert images
x=  70.9 y= 667.0 x1= 524.4 sz=[10.9] ['URWPalladioL-Roma'] | into visual embeddings that are processed jointly with text embeddings, with multimodal data
x=  70.9 y= 680.4 x1= 524.4 sz=[11.0] ['URWPalladioL-Roma'] | incorporated from the start of language-model pre-training. Overall, DeepSeek-V4.1-Flash
x=  70.9 y= 694.0 x1= 524.6 sz=[11.0] ['URWPalladioL-Roma'] | has 552B backbone parameters and 196B Engram parameters, activating 8B parameters per
x=  70.9 y= 707.5 x1= 524.4 sz=[11.0] ['URWPalladioL-Roma'] | token during prefill and 16B during decode. Figure 3 illustrates the overall architecture of
x=  70.9 y= 721.2 x1= 175.7 sz=[10.9] ['URWPalladioL-Roma'] | DeepSeek-V4.1-Flash.
x=  87.8 y= 741.6 x1= 525.5 sz=[10.8] ['URWPalladioL-Roma'] | The Causal Encoder–Decoder (CED) architecture and Compressed Sparse Attention 2 (CSA2)
x= 294.9 y= 778.9 x1= 300.4 sz=[10.9] ['URWPalladioL-Roma'] | 7

========== PAGE 8 (draw=0) ==========
x=  70.9 y=  88.1 x1= 524.4 sz=[11.0] ['URWPalladioL-Roma'] | address complementary costs of long-context inference. CED constructs the decoder’s global
x=  70.9 y= 101.8 x1= 524.6 sz=[10.8] ['URWPalladioL-Roma'] | key-value (KV) cache from encoder outputs, allowing most prompt tokens to bypass full decoder
x=  70.9 y= 115.2 x1= 524.4 sz=[11.0] ['URWPalladioL-Roma'] | computation while retaining layer-local sliding-window attention. This nearly halves prefill
x=  70.9 y= 128.7 x1= 524.4 sz=[11.0] ['URWPalladioL-Roma'] | computation, lowering the cost of processing new or uncached inputs in agentic workloads
x=  70.4 y= 142.4 x1= 524.4 sz=[10.8] ['URWPalladioL-Roma'] | with growing contexts. CSA2 shares global KV across layers to reduce cache storage and reuses
x=  70.9 y= 156.0 x1= 524.4 sz=[10.8] ['URWPalladioL-Roma'] | sparse selections to reduce indexing work. In the decoder, a Hierarchical Sparse Indexer restricts
x=  70.9 y= 169.5 x1= 524.4 sz=[10.8] ['URWPalladioL-Roma'] | later indexers to a candidate pool selected by an earlier indexer, further reducing the number of
x=  70.9 y= 183.0 x1= 188.8 sz=[10.9] ['URWPalladioL-Roma'] | entries scored per query.
x=  87.8 y= 203.4 x1= 524.4 sz=[10.8] ['URWPalladioL-Roma'] | We retain the shared and fine-grained routed experts of DeepSeekMoE (Dai et al., 2024), and
x=  70.9 y= 217.0 x1= 526.2 sz=[10.8] ['URWPalladioL-Roma'] | introduce modality-specific load balancing (Wang et al., 2024a) for image and text tokens. Single-
x=  70.9 y= 230.3 x1= 525.8 sz=[10.8] ['URWPalladioL-Ital', 'URWPalladioL-Roma'] | Pass mHC (Xie et al., 2026) revises residual-stream mixing to enable more efficient kernel fusion,
x=  70.9 y= 243.9 x1= 524.4 sz=[11.0] ['URWPalladioL-Roma'] | and Engram (Cheng et al., 2026b) adds sparsely accessed conditional memory. We omit the
x=  70.9 y= 257.6 x1= 524.4 sz=[10.8] ['URWPalladioL-Roma'] | MTP module during backbone pre-training and use DSpark (Cheng et al., 2026a) for speculative
x=  70.9 y= 271.1 x1= 524.4 sz=[11.0] ['URWPalladioL-Roma'] | decoding. We train DSpark separately after the backbone pre-training stage. Additionally, we
x=  70.9 y= 284.7 x1= 524.4 sz=[10.9] ['URWPalladioL-Roma'] | compress the main KV cache to FP4 to further reduce storage overhead. The following sections
x=  70.9 y= 298.2 x1= 421.5 sz=[10.9] ['URWPalladioL-Roma'] | describe these components and the corresponding optimization changes.
x=  70.9 y= 328.5 x1= 222.0 sz=[10.9] ['URWPalladioL-BoldItal'] | 2.1.1. Multimodal Architecture
x=  70.5 y= 351.0 x1= 524.4 sz=[11.0] ['URWPalladioL-Roma'] | The multimodal input pathway comprises a vision encoder and an MLP projector. For each
x=  70.9 y= 363.5 x1= 526.2 sz=[10.9, 11.0] ['URWPalladioL-Roma', 'txsys'] | input image, the vision encoder produces a spatial grid of visual features. A 3 × 3 pixel-
x=  70.9 y= 378.1 x1= 525.8 sz=[11.0] ['URWPalladioL-Roma'] | unshuffle operation then rearranges each local neighborhood along the channel dimension,
x=  70.9 y= 391.6 x1= 524.4 sz=[11.0] ['URWPalladioL-Roma'] | reducing the spatial resolution before the MLP projector maps the features to the hidden
x=  70.9 y= 405.3 x1= 524.4 sz=[10.8] ['URWPalladioL-Roma'] | dimension of the language backbone. Finally, the resulting visual embeddings are inserted at the
x=  70.9 y= 418.8 x1= 524.8 sz=[11.0] ['URWPalladioL-Roma'] | corresponding image-token positions in the input embedding sequence and processed jointly
x=  70.4 y= 432.4 x1= 306.9 sz=[10.9] ['URWPalladioL-Roma'] | with text embeddings by the language backbone.
x=  70.9 y= 467.8 x1= 145.7 sz=[11.0] ['URWPalladioL-Bold'] | DeepSeek-ViT
x= 156.5 y= 467.9 x1= 524.8 sz=[11.0] ['URWPalladioL-Roma'] | We train a vision encoder named DeepSeek-ViT from scratch to natively
x=  70.5 y= 481.6 x1= 526.2 sz=[10.8] ['URWPalladioL-Roma'] | process images at varying resolutions. We build DeepSeek-ViT on the Vision Transformer (Doso-
x=  70.6 y= 495.1 x1= 524.8 sz=[10.9] ['URWPalladioL-Roma'] | vitskiy et al., 2021) architecture with several modifications. To accommodate inputs of arbitrary
x=  70.9 y= 508.5 x1= 524.4 sz=[11.0] ['URWPalladioL-Roma'] | resolutions, we replace standard absolute positional embeddings with 2D-RoPE. To align the
x=  70.5 y= 522.2 x1= 526.2 sz=[10.8] ['URWPalladioL-Roma'] | ViT more closely with LLM design principles, we replace the patch embedding layer’s convolu-
x=  70.9 y= 535.6 x1= 524.4 sz=[11.0] ['URWPalladioL-Roma'] | tion with a linear projection to ensure compatibility with the Muon optimizer. We also adopt
x=  70.9 y= 549.2 x1= 524.4 sz=[11.0] ['URWPalladioL-Roma'] | RMSNorm (Zhang and Sennrich, 2019) for normalization and SwiGLU (Shazeer, 2020) as the
x=  70.9 y= 562.8 x1= 526.2 sz=[10.9] ['URWPalladioL-Roma'] | activation function. Before feeding visual features into the LLM, we apply a pixel-unshuffle op-
x=  70.9 y= 575.2 x1= 524.8 sz=[10.8, 10.9] ['URWPalladioL-Roma', 'txsys'] | eration with 3 × 3 downsampling to reduce the visual token count by a factor of nine, effectively
x=  70.9 y= 588.7 x1= 402.4 sz=[10.9] ['URWPalladioL-Roma', 'txsys'] | supporting input resolutions up to approximately 1344 × 1344 pixels.
x=  70.9 y= 625.3 x1= 365.1 sz=[11.0] ['URWPalladioL-Bold'] | Multimodal Auxiliary-loss-free Load Balancing for MoEs
x= 376.0 y= 625.4 x1= 524.4 sz=[11.0] ['URWPalladioL-Roma'] | Image and text tokens exhibit
x=  70.9 y= 638.9 x1= 524.4 sz=[11.0] ['URWPalladioL-Roma'] | distinct representation distributions and may induce different expert-routing preferences in
x=  70.9 y= 652.5 x1= 526.3 sz=[11.0] ['URWPalladioL-Roma'] | MoEs. Balancing their aggregate load may therefore obscure modality-specific imbalance.
x=  70.5 y= 666.0 x1= 524.8 sz=[11.0] ['URWPalladioL-Roma'] | To address this issue, we extend auxiliary-loss-free load balancing (Wang et al., 2024a) by
x=  70.9 y= 679.6 x1= 525.8 sz=[11.0] ['URWPalladioL-Roma'] | maintaining separate expert-wise correction biases for text and image tokens. During routing,
x=  70.9 y= 693.1 x1= 524.4 sz=[11.0] ['URWPalladioL-Roma'] | each token uses the correction biases associated with its modality for expert selection, while
x=  70.9 y= 706.7 x1= 524.4 sz=[11.0] ['URWPalladioL-Roma'] | the original routing scores are retained for weighting the selected expert outputs. After each
x=  70.9 y= 720.2 x1= 524.4 sz=[11.0] ['URWPalladioL-Roma'] | training step, the two sets of biases are updated independently according to their respective
x=  70.9 y= 733.8 x1= 524.4 sz=[11.0] ['URWPalladioL-Roma'] | expert loads. This design balances expert utilization within each modality and contributes to
x=  70.9 y= 747.4 x1= 264.9 sz=[10.9] ['URWPalladioL-Roma'] | stable and efficient multimodal training.
x= 294.9 y= 778.9 x1= 300.4 sz=[10.9] ['URWPalladioL-Roma'] | 8

========== PAGE 9 (draw=1) ==========
x=  70.9 y=  88.1 x1= 248.8 sz=[10.9] ['URWPalladioL-Bold'] | 2.2. Causal Encoder-Decoder (CED)
x=  70.9 y= 110.4 x1= 524.4 sz=[11.0] ['URWPalladioL-Roma'] | In agentic workflows, frequent tool calls generate extensive prefill requests, imposing severe
x=  70.9 y= 124.0 x1= 524.4 sz=[10.9] ['URWPalladioL-Roma'] | computational overhead when KV caches miss. To alleviate this prefill bottleneck, we propose
x=  70.9 y= 137.5 x1= 524.4 sz=[11.0] ['URWPalladioL-Roma'] | the Causal Encoder-Decoder (CED) architecture, inspired by YoCo (Sun et al., 2024). YoCo
x=  70.9 y= 151.1 x1= 524.8 sz=[11.0] ['URWPalladioL-Roma'] | reduces prefill computation by allowing the upper half of the layers to directly share the KV
x=  70.9 y= 164.6 x1= 524.4 sz=[11.0] ['URWPalladioL-Roma'] | cache generated by the lower half. Building upon this concept, CED introduces a series of
x=  70.9 y= 178.3 x1= 524.4 sz=[10.9] ['URWPalladioL-Roma'] | structural improvements to enhance both the overall KV cache capacity and the computational
x=  70.9 y= 191.7 x1= 524.4 sz=[11.0] ['URWPalladioL-Roma'] | depth of KV generation. Consequently, CED successfully reduces nearly half of the prefill
x=  70.9 y= 205.3 x1= 422.1 sz=[10.9] ['URWPalladioL-Roma'] | computation while maintaining performance comparable to the baseline.
x=  87.8 y= 223.6 x1= 524.4 sz=[10.0, 10.9, 11.0] ['URWPalladioL-Roma', 'XCharterMathMI', 'txsys'] | For global attention, CED treats the bottom 𝐿/2 layers of the Transformer as the causal
x=  70.9 y= 237.1 x1= 524.4 sz=[10.0, 10.8, 10.9] ['URWPalladioL-Roma', 'XCharterMathMI', 'txsys'] | encoder. For the upper half layers (i.e., the decoder, 𝑙> 𝐿/2), the KV entries are not derived from
x=  70.9 y= 250.7 x1= 524.4 sz=[7.3, 10.0, 11.0] ['URWPalladioL-Roma', 'XCharterMathMI'] | their respective hidden states 𝐻𝑙. Instead, they are projected directly from the hidden state of

x=  70.9 y= 337.3 x1= 524.4 sz=[11.0] ['URWPalladioL-Roma'] | tively. This design allows CED to compute only the first half of the layers during the prefill
x=  70.5 y= 351.0 x1= 473.9 sz=[10.9] ['URWPalladioL-Roma'] | phase, acquiring the upper-layer global KV cache with minimal computational cost.
x=  87.8 y= 371.3 x1= 526.2 sz=[10.9] ['URWPalladioL-Roma'] | For sliding window attention (SWA), CED maintains the conventional layer-wise computa-
x=  70.9 y= 382.7 x1= 524.8 sz=[10.0, 10.9] ['URWPalladioL-Roma', 'XCharterMathMI'] | tion across all layers. Specifically, for any layer 𝑙, the local keys and values are derived directly
x=  70.9 y= 396.3 x1= 524.4 sz=[7.3, 10.0, 11.0] ['URWPalladioL-Roma', 'XCharterMathMI'] | from the current layer’s hidden state 𝐻𝑙. This design effectively increases the computational
x=  70.9 y= 411.9 x1= 524.4 sz=[11.0] ['URWPalladioL-Roma'] | depth of local KV generation. However, maintaining this layer-wise computation necessitates
x=  70.9 y= 425.5 x1= 524.6 sz=[10.8] ['URWPalladioL-Roma'] | an SWA replay process. During the prefill phase, computing the SWA KV cache for the decoder
x=  70.9 y= 436.9 x1= 524.6 sz=[8.0, 10.0, 10.9] ['URWPalladioL-Roma', 'XCharterMathMI', 'txsys'] | requires processing an additional 𝑛win × 𝐿/2 tokens (where 𝑛win denotes the window size). For
x=  70.9 y= 452.7 x1= 524.6 sz=[10.8] ['URWPalladioL-Roma'] | multi-turn interactions with short prompts per turn, this computational overhead in the decoder
x=  70.9 y= 466.1 x1= 524.4 sz=[11.0] ['URWPalladioL-Roma'] | becomes non-negligible. Fortunately, prior work (Chen et al., 2025) has shown that the actual
x=  70.9 y= 477.6 x1= 524.8 sz=[8.0, 10.0, 10.9, 11.0] ['URWPalladioL-Roma', 'XCharterMathMI', 'txsys'] | effective receptive field of SWA is much smaller than the theoretical 𝑛win × 𝐿/2. Motivated by
x=  70.9 y= 491.1 x1= 523.9 sz=[8.0, 10.0, 10.9] ['URWPalladioL-Roma', 'XCharterMathMI'] | this observation, we introduce Decoder SWA Bounded Replay, which only prefills the last 𝑛win
x=  70.9 y= 506.9 x1= 524.4 sz=[10.8] ['URWPalladioL-Roma'] | tokens of the prompt for the SWA computation, thereby significantly reducing the computational
x=  70.9 y= 520.3 x1= 308.8 sz=[10.9] ['URWPalladioL-Roma'] | cost. Further details are provided in Section 3.2.2.
x=  87.8 y= 538.6 x1= 524.4 sz=[8.0, 10.0, 10.9] ['URWPalladioL-Roma', 'XCharterMathMI', 'txsys'] | Overall, for a sequence length 𝑁≫𝑛win, CED reduces the prefill complexity from O(𝑁𝐿) to
x=  71.1 y= 552.1 x1= 440.3 sz=[8.0, 10.0, 10.9] ['URWPalladioL-Roma', 'XCharterMathMI', 'txsys'] | O(𝑁𝐿/2 + 𝑛win × 𝐿/2) ≈O(𝑁𝐿/2), effectively halving the overall computation.
x=  70.9 y= 587.2 x1= 287.8 sz=[10.9] ['URWPalladioL-Bold'] | 2.3. Compressed Sparse Attention 2 (CSA2)
x=  70.9 y= 609.6 x1= 526.3 sz=[11.0] ['URWPalladioL-Roma'] | Serving long contexts requires controlling both KV cache storage and attention computation.
x=  70.5 y= 623.1 x1= 524.4 sz=[11.0] ['URWPalladioL-Roma'] | These costs can be reduced along three multiplicative dimensions: the entry size, where
x=  70.9 y= 636.6 x1= 525.5 sz=[11.0] ['URWPalladioL-Roma'] | GQA (Ainslie et al., 2023) reduces the number of KV heads and MLA (DeepSeek-AI, 2024)
x=  70.9 y= 648.2 x1= 526.2 sz=[10.0, 11.0] ['URWPalladioL-Roma', 'XCharterMathMI'] | shares a small latent across heads; the sequence dimension, where every 𝑚tokens are com-
x=  70.5 y= 663.9 x1= 524.6 sz=[10.9] ['URWPalladioL-Roma'] | pressed into one entry, like CSA and HCA in DeepSeek-V4 (DeepSeek-AI, 2026b); and the layer
x=  70.9 y= 677.3 x1= 524.6 sz=[11.0] ['URWPalladioL-Roma'] | dimension, where some layers reuse the caches (Brandon et al., 2024) and selections of other
x=  70.9 y= 690.8 x1= 524.6 sz=[11.0] ['URWPalladioL-Roma'] | layers instead of keeping their own, or are replaced altogether by more efficient layers. Prior
x=  70.4 y= 704.5 x1= 525.8 sz=[10.8] ['URWPalladioL-Roma'] | work has shown that compression along the layer dimension is effective: IndexCache (Bai et al.,
x=  70.9 y= 717.9 x1= 525.5 sz=[11.0] ['URWPalladioL-Roma'] | 2026) reuses Top-K indices across layers to cut indexer computation; YOIO (Sun et al., 2026b)
x=  70.9 y= 731.6 x1= 525.5 sz=[10.8] ['URWPalladioL-Roma'] | computes the sparse routing once and shares it across all layers; and HySparse (Gao et al., 2026)
x=  70.9 y= 745.2 x1= 524.4 sz=[10.8] ['URWPalladioL-Roma'] | lets sparse layers reuse the KV cache of dense layers. However, index reuse alone saves no main

x=  70.9 y= 239.8 x1= 525.8 sz=[10.9, 11.0] ['URWPalladioL-Bold', 'URWPalladioL-Roma', 'txsys'] | Figure 4 | Three operating modes of CSA2. The modes differ in how they obtain main KV,

x=  70.9 y= 330.0 x1= 524.4 sz=[11.0] ['URWPalladioL-Roma'] | KV storage, network-wide routing sharing limits performance, and hybrid designs still retain
x=  70.9 y= 343.5 x1= 524.4 sz=[11.0] ['URWPalladioL-Roma'] | full attention layers; more importantly, none of these methods covers all three multiplicative
x=  70.9 y= 357.2 x1= 129.4 sz=[10.9] ['URWPalladioL-Roma'] | dimensions.
x=  87.8 y= 377.4 x1= 524.4 sz=[11.0] ['URWPalladioL-Roma'] | CSA2 exploits the three dimensions jointly: it shares main KV and indexer K across layers
x=  70.9 y= 391.0 x1= 524.4 sz=[11.0] ['URWPalladioL-Roma'] | and allows layers to reuse Top-K indices, with cache sharing and index reuse decoupled. It
x=  70.9 y= 404.6 x1= 524.6 sz=[10.8] ['URWPalladioL-Roma'] | combines these reuse strategies with a simplified compressor and a Hierarchical Sparse Indexer
x=  70.9 y= 418.1 x1= 445.9 sz=[10.9] ['URWPalladioL-Roma'] | that narrows the search domain of subsequent indexing layers in the Decoder.
x=  87.8 y= 438.5 x1= 524.4 sz=[10.9] ['URWPalladioL-Roma'] | Similar to CSA, CSA2 includes a lightweight indexer that scores the main KV entries using
x=  70.9 y= 451.9 x1= 524.4 sz=[11.0] ['URWPalladioL-Roma'] | indexer Q and indexer K and selects the Top-K entries for each query. Each Q attends to the
x=  70.9 y= 465.6 x1= 524.4 sz=[10.8] ['URWPalladioL-Roma'] | selected entries together with the layer-local sliding-window KV (SWA KV). CSA2 also includes
x=  70.9 y= 479.1 x1= 525.8 sz=[10.9] ['URWPalladioL-Roma'] | the uncompressed main KV setting as a special case with a compression ratio of 1. Meanwhile,
x=  70.9 y= 490.6 x1= 524.4 sz=[10.0, 10.8] ['URWPalladioL-Roma', 'XCharterMathMI'] | CSA2 simplifies both the compressor and the indexer. In CSA, a compression ratio of 𝑚produces
x=  70.9 y= 504.1 x1= 524.6 sz=[10.0, 11.0] ['URWPalladioL-Roma', 'XCharterMathMI'] | each main KV entry from 2𝑚original KV cache entries, with overlapping source entries for
x=  70.9 y= 519.7 x1= 524.4 sz=[11.0] ['URWPalladioL-Roma'] | adjacent compressed entries. It also includes absolute positional embedding to encode the
x=  70.5 y= 531.2 x1= 524.4 sz=[10.0, 11.0] ['URWPalladioL-Roma', 'XCharterMathMI'] | positions of these 2𝑚entries during compression. CSA2 removes this overlap and absolute
x=  70.5 y= 546.8 x1= 525.8 sz=[11.0] ['URWPalladioL-Roma'] | positional embedding. In addition, CSA2 obtains indexer K by projecting main KV entries,
x=  70.9 y= 560.3 x1= 524.4 sz=[11.0] ['URWPalladioL-Roma'] | replacing CSA’s separate compression path from hidden states. Both designs simplify the
x=  70.9 y= 574.0 x1= 319.8 sz=[10.9] ['URWPalladioL-Roma'] | implementation and increase the training efficiency.
x=  87.8 y= 594.3 x1= 524.4 sz=[10.9] ['URWPalladioL-Roma'] | Sections 2.3.1 and 2.3.2 describe the cross-layer reuse strategies and the Hierarchical Sparse

x=  70.9 y= 389.7 x1= 202.1 sz=[10.9] ['URWPalladioL-Roma'] | CSA layer in DeepSeek-V4.
x=  87.8 y= 410.0 x1= 524.6 sz=[10.9] ['URWPalladioL-Bold', 'URWPalladioL-Roma'] | Reindex Mode. The layer reuses the most recent available main KV from a preceding layer
x=  70.9 y= 423.5 x1= 524.4 sz=[10.9, 11.0] ['URWPalladioL-Roma'] | together with its corresponding indexer K. The indexer computes its own query, rescores the
x=  70.9 y= 437.2 x1= 524.4 sz=[10.8] ['URWPalladioL-Roma'] | reused keys, and produces fresh Top-K indices. This allows the sparse selection to change across
x=  70.9 y= 450.7 x1= 321.0 sz=[10.9] ['URWPalladioL-Roma'] | layers while main KV and indexer K remain shared.
x=  87.8 y= 470.9 x1= 524.8 sz=[10.9, 11.0] ['URWPalladioL-Bold', 'URWPalladioL-Roma'] | Reuse Mode. The layer reuses the most recent available main KV and the latest Top-K
x=  70.9 y= 484.5 x1= 524.4 sz=[10.9, 11.0] ['URWPalladioL-Roma'] | indices computed against that main KV by a preceding layer in Full or Reindex Mode. It
x=  70.5 y= 498.2 x1= 526.3 sz=[10.8] ['URWPalladioL-Roma'] | performs attention using this selection without computing indexer Q or evaluating index scores.
x=  87.8 y= 518.5 x1= 524.4 sz=[10.9] ['URWPalladioL-Roma'] | Sharing main KV and indexer K reduces cache storage, while reusing Top-K indices avoids
x=  70.9 y= 531.9 x1= 524.4 sz=[11.0] ['URWPalladioL-Roma'] | additional indexer computation. Reindex Mode preserves cache sharing while allowing the
x=  70.9 y= 545.6 x1= 524.6 sz=[10.9] ['URWPalladioL-Roma'] | selected entries to change across layers. When CSA2 is combined with CED, the decoder layer
x=  70.9 y= 557.0 x1= 525.8 sz=[10.0, 10.9] ['URWPalladioL-Roma', 'XCharterMathMI', 'txsys'] | assigned to Full Mode computes its own global KV from the hidden state of the (𝐿/2)-th layer,
x=  70.9 y= 572.7 x1= 485.2 sz=[10.9] ['URWPalladioL-Roma'] | i.e. the last layer of the causal encoder. The Reindex and Reuse Modes are unchanged.
x=  70.9 y= 603.0 x1= 234.6 sz=[10.9] ['URWPalladioL-BoldItal'] | 2.3.2. Hierarchical Sparse Indexer
x=  70.9 y= 625.6 x1= 524.4 sz=[10.9] ['URWPalladioL-Roma'] | Cross-layer index reuse reduces the number of indexer evaluations, but the remaining indexers
x=  70.9 y= 639.0 x1= 524.4 sz=[11.0] ['URWPalladioL-Roma'] | still score the full causally visible context. For extremely long contexts, this cost remains a
x=  70.9 y= 652.7 x1= 524.4 sz=[10.8] ['URWPalladioL-Roma'] | major computational bottleneck. Prior work introduced indexer sparsity by scoring and pruning
x=  70.5 y= 666.2 x1= 524.4 sz=[10.9] ['URWPalladioL-Roma'] | pooled block representations before token-level indexing (Xu et al., 2026b). We find that in the
x=  70.9 y= 679.7 x1= 524.4 sz=[11.0] ['URWPalladioL-Roma'] | decoder, information from shallower indexers can naturally be used to restrict the candidates
x=  70.9 y= 693.2 x1= 524.4 sz=[11.0] ['URWPalladioL-Roma'] | considered by deeper indexers without adding any extra state. We therefore introduce the
x=  70.9 y= 706.8 x1= 524.4 sz=[10.9] ['URWPalladioL-Roma'] | Hierarchical Sparse Indexer, which is used only in the decoder of CED to reduce this repeated
x=  70.9 y= 720.3 x1= 524.4 sz=[11.0] ['URWPalladioL-Roma'] | scoring during decode. For each query, the first layer assigned to Full Mode constructs a
x=  70.9 y= 733.9 x1= 526.2 sz=[11.0] ['URWPalladioL-Roma'] | candidate pool that later re-indexing layers use as their search domain. For a fixed candidate-
x=  70.5 y= 747.4 x1= 524.4 sz=[11.0] ['URWPalladioL-Roma'] | pool size, this changes the per-query cost of deeper indexers from linear in context length to
x= 292.2 y= 778.9 x1= 303.1 sz=[10.9] ['URWPalladioL-Roma'] | 11

========== PAGE 12 (draw=0) ==========
x=  70.9 y=  88.1 x1= 524.4 sz=[11.0] ['URWPalladioL-Roma'] | constant. The mechanism is training-aware and introduced in post-training: the candidate
x=  70.9 y= 101.8 x1= 524.4 sz=[10.8] ['URWPalladioL-Roma'] | restriction is applied identically during training and inference, so deeper indexers are optimized
x=  70.9 y= 115.3 x1= 478.0 sz=[10.9] ['URWPalladioL-Roma'] | under the same search domain they use at inference. Figure 5 illustrates this process.
x=  87.8 y= 135.5 x1= 524.4 sz=[11.0] ['URWPalladioL-Roma'] | This first Full Mode layer scores all causally visible main KV positions and produces the
x=  70.5 y= 149.1 x1= 524.7 sz=[10.9] ['URWPalladioL-Roma'] | Top-K indices for its own attention. It also performs blockwise candidate selection: each block
x=  70.9 y= 162.6 x1= 524.4 sz=[11.0] ['URWPalladioL-Roma'] | is assigned the maximum index score among its positions, and the blocks with the highest
x=  70.9 y= 176.3 x1= 524.4 sz=[10.9] ['URWPalladioL-Roma'] | scores are selected. It then collects the positions covered by the selected blocks into a candidate
x=  70.5 y= 189.7 x1= 524.4 sz=[11.0] ['URWPalladioL-Roma'] | pool larger than the final Top-K set. For example, selecting 2,048 blocks with 8 positions each
x=  70.6 y= 203.4 x1= 524.8 sz=[10.8] ['URWPalladioL-Roma'] | yields 16,384 candidate positions. This pool defines where later indexers search; the final Top-K
x=  70.9 y= 216.9 x1= 366.4 sz=[10.9] ['URWPalladioL-Roma'] | selection determines which main KV entries each layer reads.
x=  87.8 y= 237.3 x1= 524.4 sz=[10.8] ['URWPalladioL-Roma'] | Subsequent layers in Reindex Mode score only the candidate positions for the corresponding
x=  70.6 y= 250.7 x1= 524.4 sz=[11.0] ['URWPalladioL-Roma'] | query and select their own Top-K entries within that pool. Layers in Reuse Mode perform no
x=  70.9 y= 264.4 x1= 525.8 sz=[10.8] ['URWPalladioL-Roma'] | new indexing and use the latest Top-K indices computed against the main KV they reuse. Thus,
x=  70.9 y= 277.9 x1= 497.5 sz=[10.9] ['URWPalladioL-Roma'] | the candidate pool is shared across indexing layers, while their final selections can differ.
x=  87.8 y= 298.3 x1= 524.4 sz=[10.8] ['URWPalladioL-Roma'] | For a fixed candidate-pool size, the number of positions scored per query by each subsequent
x=  70.9 y= 311.7 x1= 524.4 sz=[11.0] ['URWPalladioL-Roma'] | indexer is bounded independently of context length. The first Full Mode layer still scans the
x=  70.9 y= 325.2 x1= 524.6 sz=[11.0] ['URWPalladioL-Roma'] | entire causally visible range. Hierarchical indexing therefore reduces the cost of later indexer
x=  70.9 y= 338.8 x1= 328.2 sz=[10.9] ['URWPalladioL-Roma'] | evaluations while retaining the initial full-range pass.

x=  70.9 y= 581.8 x1= 524.4 sz=[10.9, 11.0] ['URWPalladioL-Roma', 'txsys'] | orders {2, 3, 4}, with 8 hash heads and a total embedding dimension of 2048 per order. Each
x=  70.9 y= 596.6 x1= 526.3 sz=[10.8] ['URWPalladioL-Roma'] | head indexes a table of approximately 16M entries, with table sizes chosen to be distinct primes.
x=  70.9 y= 610.1 x1= 524.4 sz=[11.0] ['URWPalladioL-Roma'] | Both the embedding tables and the key/value projections use FP8 precision. The modules are
x=  70.5 y= 623.7 x1= 526.3 sz=[10.8] ['URWPalladioL-Roma'] | placed at layers 1 and 14 (zero-indexed) to balance memory usage across training pipeline stages.
x=  70.9 y= 637.1 x1= 524.4 sz=[11.0] ['URWPalladioL-Roma'] | During inference, deterministic addressing enables embeddings to be prefetched from host

x= 292.2 y= 778.9 x1= 303.1 sz=[10.9] ['URWPalladioL-Roma'] | 13

========== PAGE 14 (draw=2) ==========
x=  70.5 y=  88.2 x1= 526.3 sz=[10.9] ['URWPalladioL-Roma'] | The drafter comprises three Transformer blocks with a sliding attention window of 128 tokens.
x=  70.4 y= 101.6 x1= 524.4 sz=[11.0] ['URWPalladioL-Roma'] | A single forward pass through these blocks computes base logits for five draft positions in
x=  70.5 y= 115.2 x1= 524.8 sz=[11.0] ['URWPalladioL-Roma'] | parallel, while a lightweight Markov head models dependencies among the draft tokens. A
x=  70.9 y= 128.8 x1= 524.4 sz=[11.0] ['URWPalladioL-Roma'] | confidence head predicts per-position conditional acceptance probabilities, which are used to
x=  70.9 y= 142.3 x1= 524.4 sz=[11.0] ['URWPalladioL-Roma'] | estimate prefix survival probabilities. The scheduler combines these estimates with profiled
x=  70.9 y= 156.0 x1= 524.4 sz=[10.9] ['URWPalladioL-Roma'] | engine throughput curves to dynamically select the verification length for each request, aiming
x=  70.9 y= 169.5 x1= 479.2 sz=[10.9] ['URWPalladioL-Roma'] | to maximize expected system-wide token throughput under the current system load.
x=  87.8 y= 189.7 x1= 524.8 sz=[11.0] ['URWPalladioL-Roma'] | Unlike the MTP module in DeepSeek-V3 (DeepSeek-AI, 2024), which is trained jointly
x=  70.4 y= 203.3 x1= 524.6 sz=[11.0] ['URWPalladioL-Roma'] | with the backbone throughout pre-training, DSpark is introduced in a dedicated stage after
x=  70.5 y= 217.0 x1= 526.2 sz=[10.8] ['URWPalladioL-Roma'] | pre-training. In this stage, we train only DSpark while keeping the backbone frozen. During post-
x=  70.9 y= 230.4 x1= 524.4 sz=[11.0] ['URWPalladioL-Roma'] | training, we continue to train DSpark alongside the backbone, without propagating gradients
x=  70.9 y= 243.9 x1= 524.4 sz=[11.0] ['URWPalladioL-Roma'] | from the DSpark objective into the backbone. This keeps DSpark aligned with the evolving
x=  70.5 y= 257.5 x1= 508.7 sz=[10.9] ['URWPalladioL-Roma'] | policy, enabling it to accelerate both online serving and rollout generation for RL and OPD.
x=  70.9 y= 286.6 x1= 198.3 sz=[10.9] ['URWPalladioL-BoldItal'] | 2.4.4. FP4 Main KV Cache
x=  70.9 y= 309.1 x1= 526.3 sz=[11.0] ['URWPalladioL-Roma'] | Long-context agent workloads require large per-request KV caches, increasing serving costs.
x=  70.9 y= 322.8 x1= 524.6 sz=[10.8] ['URWPalladioL-Roma'] | DeepSeek-V4 already uses quantization-aware training (QAT) (Jacob et al., 2018) for FP4 indexer
x=  70.6 y= 336.3 x1= 524.4 sz=[10.8] ['URWPalladioL-Roma'] | queries and keys, accelerating index computation and reducing the indexer cache size. We adopt
x=  70.9 y= 349.8 x1= 524.4 sz=[10.8] ['URWPalladioL-Roma'] | the OCP-standard MXFP4 format (Rouhani et al., 2023) to support as many hardware platforms
x=  70.9 y= 363.3 x1= 524.9 sz=[11.0] ['URWPalladioL-Roma'] | as possible, despite the higher accuracy of alternative formats in our experiments. We now
x=  70.9 y= 376.8 x1= 524.7 sz=[11.0] ['URWPalladioL-Roma'] | extend QAT to the main KV cache, where FP4 reduces storage rather than accelerates matrix
x=  70.9 y= 390.4 x1= 524.4 sz=[11.0] ['URWPalladioL-Roma'] | multiplication. Dequantizing cached values before attention allows us to use a more accurate
x=  70.9 y= 403.9 x1= 524.4 sz=[11.0] ['URWPalladioL-Roma'] | format without requiring native matrix-multiplication support for that format, preserving
x=  70.9 y= 417.5 x1= 269.1 sz=[10.9] ['URWPalladioL-Roma'] | compatibility across hardware platforms.
x=  87.8 y= 437.9 x1= 524.4 sz=[10.9] ['URWPalladioL-Roma'] | Among the approximately four-bit formats evaluated, we select E2M1 with one E4M3 scale
x=  70.5 y= 451.3 x1= 524.4 sz=[11.0] ['URWPalladioL-Roma'] | per 16 channels, following NVFP4 (Alvarez et al., 2025) but omitting its second-level global
x=  70.9 y= 464.9 x1= 524.6 sz=[11.0] ['URWPalladioL-Roma'] | scale to balance accuracy and simplicity. Omitting this scale leaves ample dynamic range for
x=  70.9 y= 477.4 x1= 524.4 sz=[10.9] ['URWPalladioL-Roma', 'txmiaX', 'txsys'] | the main KV cache: the format supports magnitudes up to 448 × 6 = 2688, far above the cache’s
x=  70.9 y= 492.0 x1= 524.4 sz=[11.0] ['URWPalladioL-Roma'] | magnitude bound. In DeepSeek-V4.1-Flash, the largest trained RMSNorm weight magnitude
x=  70.9 y= 505.5 x1= 524.4 sz=[11.0] ['URWPalladioL-Roma'] | is approximately 1. After RMS normalization, the L2 norm of the 512-channel KV latent is at
x= 172.5 y= 508.7 x1= 180.2 sz=[10.9] ['txsys'] | √
x=  70.9 y= 519.1 x1= 169.8 sz=[11.0] ['URWPalladioL-Roma'] | most approximately
x= 180.2 y= 519.1 x1= 524.4 sz=[10.9, 11.0] ['URWPalladioL-Roma'] | 512. RoPE preserves this norm, so the maximum absolute value across
x= 351.4 y= 522.3 x1= 359.1 sz=[10.9] ['txsys'] | √
x= 359.1 y= 531.5 x1= 524.4 sz=[10.9, 11.0] ['URWPalladioL-Roma', 'txsys'] | 512 ≈22.6. Besides, the maximum
x=  70.9 y= 532.7 x1= 348.7 sz=[11.0] ['URWPalladioL-Roma'] | channels after rotation is also bounded by approximately
x=  70.9 y= 546.2 x1= 524.4 sz=[11.0] ['URWPalladioL-Roma'] | magnitude observed during training is around 10. Therefore, omitting the global scale causes
x=  70.9 y= 559.8 x1= 397.3 sz=[10.9] ['URWPalladioL-Roma'] | no measurable decrease in accuracy and simplifies the cache layout.
x=  87.8 y= 580.2 x1= 526.2 sz=[10.8] ['URWPalladioL-Roma'] | To enable FP4 main KV cache storage in DeepSeek-V4.1-Flash, we introduce QAT during post-
x=  70.9 y= 593.7 x1= 524.4 sz=[10.9] ['URWPalladioL-Roma'] | training. The non-RoPE and RoPE components use the same quantization format. We quantize
x=  70.9 y= 607.1 x1= 524.4 sz=[11.0] ['URWPalladioL-Roma'] | the cache after RoPE: quantizing before RoPE yields only a marginal accuracy improvement
x=  70.9 y= 620.8 x1= 524.4 sz=[10.9] ['URWPalladioL-Roma'] | in our experiments and would introduce additional overhead during decoding. We retain FP8
x=  70.9 y= 634.3 x1= 524.8 sz=[11.0] ['URWPalladioL-Roma'] | for the SWA KV cache due to its sensitivity to quantization. Compared with the FP8 main KV
x=  70.9 y= 647.9 x1= 524.4 sz=[10.9] ['URWPalladioL-Roma'] | cache in DeepSeek-V4, this format nearly halves the storage footprint, both in HBM and when
x=  70.9 y= 661.4 x1= 154.5 sz=[10.9] ['URWPalladioL-Roma'] | offloaded to SSD.
x=  70.9 y= 692.7 x1= 159.9 sz=[10.9] ['URWPalladioL-Bold'] | 2.5. Optimization
x=  70.9 y= 715.2 x1= 526.2 sz=[10.8] ['URWPalladioL-Roma'] | Building upon the optimization configuration used in DeepSeek-V4, we make some new modifi-
x=  70.9 y= 728.7 x1= 318.3 sz=[10.9] ['URWPalladioL-Roma'] | cations to better align with the architectural design.
x=  87.8 y= 749.0 x1= 524.4 sz=[10.9] ['URWPalladioL-Roma'] | First, we use head-wise Muon, where Query weights are split by head before applying the
x= 292.2 y= 778.9 x1= 303.1 sz=[10.9] ['URWPalladioL-Roma'] | 14

========== PAGE 15 (draw=5) ==========
x=  70.9 y=  88.2 x1= 524.4 sz=[10.8] ['URWPalladioL-Roma'] | Muon update. Here, we briefly discuss the motivation for such a design. By viewing Muon as a
x=  70.5 y= 101.7 x1= 524.4 sz=[10.9] ['URWPalladioL-Roma'] | preconditioned gradient descent, vanilla Muon uses one preconditioner for all heads, whereas
x=  70.9 y= 115.3 x1= 524.6 sz=[10.9] ['URWPalladioL-Roma'] | head-wise Muon provides different preconditioners for different heads. This design can better
x=  70.9 y= 128.9 x1= 524.4 sz=[10.8] ['URWPalladioL-Roma'] | handle the heterogeneity across attention heads (Zhang et al., 2024; Zhang, 2026, Section 3). As a
x=  70.9 y= 142.4 x1= 524.4 sz=[10.8] ['URWPalladioL-Roma'] | result, we observe that head-wise Muon outperforms vanilla Muon. The empirical advantage of
x=  70.9 y= 156.0 x1= 526.1 sz=[10.8] ['URWPalladioL-Roma'] | head-wise Muon is also validated in GLM 5 (Zeng et al., 2026) and Kimi-K3 (Team et al., 2026a).
x=  87.8 y= 176.3 x1= 524.4 sz=[10.8] ['URWPalladioL-Roma'] | Second, applying Adam to the newly introduced Engram parameters substantially increases
x=  70.9 y= 189.9 x1= 526.2 sz=[10.8] ['URWPalladioL-Roma'] | the optimizer-state memory footprint. To reduce memory usage during training, we instead opti-
x=  70.9 y= 203.4 x1= 526.2 sz=[10.8] ['URWPalladioL-Roma'] | mize the Engram embedding tables, token embedding, and prediction head using a momentum-
x=  70.9 y= 217.0 x1= 524.4 sz=[10.8] ['URWPalladioL-Roma'] | based update followed by Sinkhorn balancing. Sinkhorn balancing has previously been applied
x=  70.9 y= 230.4 x1= 524.4 sz=[11.0] ['URWPalladioL-Roma'] | to linear-layer weight matrices in SinkGD (Scetbon et al., 2025); here, we extend it to these
x=  70.9 y= 243.9 x1= 524.4 sz=[11.0] ['URWPalladioL-Roma'] | large parameter matrices. Like Muon, this approach requires only a momentum buffer while
x=  70.9 y= 257.5 x1= 235.4 sz=[10.9] ['URWPalladioL-Roma'] | empirically outperforming Adam.
x=  70.9 y= 292.9 x1= 179.3 sz=[11.0] ['URWPalladioL-Bold'] | Basic Configurations.
x= 190.2 y= 293.0 x1= 526.2 sz=[11.0] ['URWPalladioL-Roma'] | We retain AdamW (Loshchilov and Hutter, 2019) for normalization-
x=  70.9 y= 306.6 x1= 524.4 sz=[11.0] ['URWPalladioL-Roma'] | layer weights and other non-matrix parameters, including biases and scaling factors. We use
x=  70.9 y= 320.1 x1= 526.2 sz=[11.0] ['URWPalladioL-Roma'] | Muon (Jordan et al., 2024) for the weight matrices of linear transformations in the language-
x=  70.9 y= 333.7 x1= 524.4 sz=[11.0] ['URWPalladioL-Roma'] | model backbone, the Engram projection layers, and the vision-language projector. We use
x=  70.9 y= 347.3 x1= 524.7 sz=[10.9] ['URWPalladioL-Roma'] | head-wise Muon for Query and Key weights. We apply decoupled weight decay and Nesterov
x=  70.9 y= 360.8 x1= 524.4 sz=[11.0] ['URWPalladioL-Roma'] | momentum to Muon (Nesterov, 1983; Liu et al., 2025); normalization-layer weights are also
x=  70.9 y= 374.3 x1= 524.4 sz=[11.0] ['URWPalladioL-Roma'] | subject to weight decay, whereas biases and scaling factors are not. The Sinkhorn-balanced
x=  70.9 y= 388.0 x1= 524.4 sz=[10.8] ['URWPalladioL-Roma'] | update also uses Nesterov momentum but does not apply weight decay. During pre-training, we
x=  70.9 y= 401.5 x1= 524.4 sz=[10.9] ['URWPalladioL-Roma'] | keep the vision encoder frozen until the learning-rate decay stage, while its final normalization
x=  70.9 y= 415.1 x1= 524.4 sz=[10.8] ['URWPalladioL-Roma'] | layer and the vision–language projector remain trainable. At the onset of learning-rate decay, we
x=  70.9 y= 428.6 x1= 521.1 sz=[10.9] ['URWPalladioL-Roma'] | unfreeze the vision encoder and optimize it jointly with the LLM with a smaller learning rate.
x=  70.9 y= 464.0 x1= 445.9 sz=[11.0] ['URWPalladioL-Bold'] | Sinkhorn-Balanced Updates for Engram / Embedding / Prediction Head.
