x=  70.9 y= 552.7 x1= 273.6 sz=[10.9] ['URWPalladioL-BoldItal'] | 3.1.1. Multimodal Training Infrastructure
x=  70.9 y= 575.2 x1= 389.1 sz=[10.9] ['URWPalladioL-Bold'] | Communication-Computation Overlap in Contrastive Learning.
x= 399.9 y= 575.3 x1= 524.4 sz=[10.9] ['URWPalladioL-Roma'] | The vision encoder is first
x=  70.9 y= 588.7 x1= 524.4 sz=[11.0] ['URWPalladioL-Roma'] | optimized with a contrastive objective before being fine-tuned with a generative next-token
x=  70.5 y= 602.3 x1= 524.4 sz=[11.0] ['URWPalladioL-Roma'] | prediction loss. In the contrastive phase, the loss is computed over a full batch of text and
x=  70.6 y= 615.9 x1= 525.8 sz=[10.9] ['URWPalladioL-Roma'] | vision pairs, so the features of both modalities must be all-gathered across data-parallel ranks,
x=  70.9 y= 629.5 x1= 524.4 sz=[10.8] ['URWPalladioL-Roma'] | incurring substantial communication. Because the gradient of the text features depends only on
x=  70.9 y= 643.0 x1= 524.4 sz=[11.0] ['URWPalladioL-Roma'] | the gathered visual features—and, symmetrically, the gradient of the visual features depends
x=  70.9 y= 656.5 x1= 524.6 sz=[11.0] ['URWPalladioL-Roma'] | only on the gathered text features—each all-gather can be overlapped with the forward or
x=  70.9 y= 670.1 x1= 293.7 sz=[10.9] ['URWPalladioL-Roma'] | backward pass instead of stalling the pipeline:
x=  80.8 y= 686.6 x1= 346.3 sz=[8.0, 10.0, 10.9] ['URWPalladioL-Roma', 'XCharterMathMI', 'txexs', 'txsys'] | Forward(𝑉) → Forward(𝑇) ∥AllGather(𝑉) →∇Text
x= 207.6 y= 703.1 x1= 514.5 sz=[8.0, 10.0, 10.9] ['URWPalladioL-Roma', 'XCharterMathMI', 'txexs', 'txsys'] | → Backward(𝑇) ∥AllGather(𝑇) →∇Vision →Backward(𝑉),
x=  70.4 y= 733.4 x1= 524.3 sz=[10.0, 10.8, 10.9] ['URWPalladioL-Roma', 'XCharterMathMI', 'txsys'] | where 𝑉and 𝑇denote the visual and text features, (𝐴∥𝐶) denotes the overlap of computation 𝐴
x=  70.4 y= 746.9 x1= 524.4 sz=[10.0, 10.9, 11.0] ['URWPalladioL-Roma', 'XCharterMathMI', 'txsys'] | with communication 𝐶, and ∇denotes the gradient computation. In this schedule, the visual
x= 292.2 y= 778.9 x1= 303.1 sz=[10.9] ['URWPalladioL-Roma'] | 16

========== PAGE 17 (draw=3) ==========
x=  70.9 y=  88.3 x1= 524.4 sz=[10.8] ['URWPalladioL-Roma'] | features are gathered during the text forward pass and the text features during the text backward
x=  70.5 y= 101.7 x1= 438.3 sz=[10.9] ['URWPalladioL-Roma'] | pass, so that both all-gathers are hidden entirely behind useful computation.
x=  70.9 y= 137.1 x1= 192.9 sz=[11.0] ['URWPalladioL-Bold'] | End-to-End Parallelism.
x= 203.8 y= 137.2 x1= 524.4 sz=[11.0] ['URWPalladioL-Roma'] | To handle the model and data heterogeneity between the vision
x=  70.9 y= 150.9 x1= 524.4 sz=[10.8] ['URWPalladioL-Roma'] | encoder and the LLM (Zhang et al., 2025b), we adopt the disaggregated encoder design used in
x=  70.9 y= 164.4 x1= 524.4 sz=[11.0] ['URWPalladioL-Roma'] | recent training systems (Team et al., 2025, 2026b). The vision encoder is replicated outside the
x=  70.9 y= 178.0 x1= 525.8 sz=[10.8] ['URWPalladioL-Roma'] | LLM parameter tree, and each training step is divided into three phases: vision encoder forward,
x=  70.9 y= 191.5 x1= 524.4 sz=[10.9] ['URWPalladioL-Roma'] | LLM forward/backward, and vision encoder backward. This separation prevents interference
x=  70.9 y= 205.1 x1= 524.4 sz=[10.9] ['URWPalladioL-Roma'] | between vision encoder and LLM computation. Load-balanced vision processing is confined to
x=  70.9 y= 218.7 x1= 524.4 sz=[10.8] ['URWPalladioL-Roma'] | the first and last phases, while the LLM phase remains free of vision computation and preserves
x=  70.9 y= 232.2 x1= 268.9 sz=[10.9] ['URWPalladioL-Roma'] | the parallel strategy of text-only training.
x=  70.9 y= 267.6 x1= 331.7 sz=[11.0] ['URWPalladioL-Bold'] | Long-Sequence Multimodal Training Optimization.
x= 342.6 y= 267.7 x1= 526.2 sz=[11.0] ['URWPalladioL-Roma'] | DeepSeek-V4.1-Flash is trained on se-
x=  70.6 y= 281.3 x1= 524.4 sz=[10.9] ['URWPalladioL-Roma'] | quences of up to one million tokens, with a substantial share of training occurring at ultra-long
x=  70.9 y= 294.8 x1= 525.8 sz=[11.0] ['URWPalladioL-Roma'] | sequence lengths in both the pre-training and post-training stages. At these sequence lengths,
x=  70.9 y= 308.4 x1= 410.4 sz=[10.9] ['URWPalladioL-Roma'] | multimodal samples create heavy I/O, CPU, and memory bottlenecks.
x=  86.1 y= 340.6 x1= 524.4 sz=[10.8, 10.9] ['URWPalladioL-Bold', 'URWPalladioL-Roma'] | • Balanced image sharding. During pre-training, a single ultra-long, image-dense sequence
x=  98.1 y= 354.1 x1= 524.4 sz=[11.0] ['URWPalladioL-Roma'] | can exhaust one host’s I/O, CPU, and memory during loading, so the images of each
x=  98.1 y= 367.8 x1= 524.4 sz=[10.9] ['URWPalladioL-Roma'] | sequence are sharded across the CP ranks with load balancing, and each image is loaded
x=  98.1 y= 381.3 x1= 511.7 sz=[10.9] ['URWPalladioL-Roma'] | exactly once. With images read once, loading stays hidden behind compute whenever
x= 225.8 y= 401.1 x1= 250.9 sz=[10.0, 10.9] ['XCharterMathMI', 'txsys'] | 𝑁× 𝜌
x= 255.3 y= 401.1 x1= 291.3 sz=[10.0, 10.9] ['XCharterMathMI', 'txsys'] | < 𝑁× 𝐶
x= 366.6 y= 401.1 x1= 381.5 sz=[8.0, 10.0] ['URWPalladioL-Roma', 'XCharterMathMI'] | 𝐵IO
x= 342.2 y= 408.5 x1= 357.5 sz=[10.0] ['XCharterMathMI'] | 𝜌<
x= 389.0 y= 408.5 x1= 398.3 sz=[10.0, 10.9] ['URWPalladioL-Roma', 'XCharterMathMI'] | 𝐶,
x= 307.0 y= 409.4 x1= 327.8 sz=[10.9] ['txsys'] | ⇐⇒
x= 230.6 y= 415.9 x1= 245.6 sz=[8.0, 10.0] ['URWPalladioL-Roma', 'XCharterMathMI'] | 𝐵IO
x= 267.4 y= 415.9 x1= 290.6 sz=[8.0, 10.0] ['URWPalladioL-Roma', 'XCharterMathMI'] | 𝐵GPU
x= 362.5 y= 415.9 x1= 385.6 sz=[8.0, 10.0] ['URWPalladioL-Roma', 'XCharterMathMI'] | 𝐵GPU
x=  97.7 y= 437.7 x1= 525.8 sz=[8.0, 10.0, 10.9] ['URWPalladioL-Roma', 'XCharterMathMI'] | where 𝑁is the token count, 𝜌the raw bytes per token, 𝐶the per-token compute, and 𝐵IO,
x=  98.7 y= 451.3 x1= 526.2 sz=[8.0, 10.0, 10.8] ['URWPalladioL-Roma', 'XCharterMathMI'] | 𝐵GPU the file-system and GPU bandwidths. Since 𝑁cancels, the criterion involves only per-
x=  98.1 y= 464.8 x1= 524.4 sz=[10.0, 10.8] ['URWPalladioL-Roma', 'XCharterMathMI'] | token quantities (𝜌and 𝐶), independent of sequence length and cluster size; 𝜌is set by the
x=  97.8 y= 480.5 x1= 524.4 sz=[10.9] ['URWPalladioL-Roma'] | vision-module configuration (e.g., the resolution cap or the spatial downsample). Storage
x=  98.1 y= 493.9 x1= 524.4 sz=[11.0] ['URWPalladioL-Roma'] | throughput therefore becomes a bottleneck only for small models with low per-token
x=  98.1 y= 507.6 x1= 488.4 sz=[10.9] ['URWPalladioL-Roma'] | compute, as in ablations, while production-scale models remain compute-bound.
x=  86.1 y= 520.9 x1= 526.2 sz=[10.9, 11.0] ['URWPalladioL-Bold', 'URWPalladioL-Roma'] | • Incremental image transfer. Besides the balanced sharding above, the reinforcement-
x=  98.1 y= 534.6 x1= 524.4 sz=[11.0] ['URWPalladioL-Roma'] | learning rollout transfers images to the inference engine only incrementally, and caches
x=  98.1 y= 548.1 x1= 524.4 sz=[11.0] ['URWPalladioL-Roma'] | the engine’s CPU-side decoding and preprocessing outputs on a distributed file system
x=  98.1 y= 561.8 x1= 335.5 sz=[10.9] ['URWPalladioL-Roma'] | for reuse across rollouts and subsequent training.
x=  70.9 y= 593.8 x1= 278.3 sz=[10.9] ['URWPalladioL-BoldItal'] | 3.1.2. Attention Sharing Training for CSA2
x=  70.9 y= 616.4 x1= 524.4 sz=[10.8] ['URWPalladioL-Roma'] | In Section 2.3, we introduce CSA2, an attention sharing method with three modes, some of which
x=  70.9 y= 630.0 x1= 524.4 sz=[10.8] ['URWPalladioL-Roma'] | involve sharing one or more of the main KV, the indexer K, and the Top-K indices across multiple
x=  70.9 y= 643.4 x1= 524.4 sz=[11.0] ['URWPalladioL-Roma'] | layers. Supporting CSA2 in large-scale distributed training requires additional coordination
x=  70.9 y= 656.9 x1= 524.4 sz=[11.0] ['URWPalladioL-Roma'] | beyond the attention computation itself. In particular, layers that share attention components
x=  70.9 y= 670.5 x1= 524.4 sz=[11.0] ['URWPalladioL-Roma'] | may be placed on different pipeline stages, making direct module reuse incompatible with
x=  70.9 y= 684.0 x1= 524.4 sz=[11.0] ['URWPalladioL-Roma'] | conventional stage-local execution. Therefore, we adopt several designs to support CSA2
x=  70.9 y= 697.6 x1= 112.0 sz=[10.9] ['URWPalladioL-Roma'] | training.
x=  87.8 y= 717.8 x1= 524.4 sz=[11.0] ['URWPalladioL-Bold', 'URWPalladioL-Roma'] | Shadow indexers address this issue by placing a lightweight executable replica on each
x=  70.5 y= 731.6 x1= 524.6 sz=[10.9] ['URWPalladioL-Roma'] | participating stage while retaining a single logical owner for the shared parameters. The owner
x=  70.9 y= 745.0 x1= 524.4 sz=[11.0] ['URWPalladioL-Roma'] | remains responsible for optimization and checkpointing, whereas parameter synchronization
x= 292.2 y= 778.9 x1= 303.1 sz=[10.9] ['URWPalladioL-Roma'] | 17

========== PAGE 18 (draw=0) ==========
x=  70.9 y=  88.2 x1= 524.4 sz=[10.8] ['URWPalladioL-Roma'] | and gradient aggregation keep the shadow replicas consistent throughout training. This design
x=  70.5 y= 101.8 x1= 524.4 sz=[10.8] ['URWPalladioL-Roma'] | preserves the original model semantics without requiring the pipeline scheduler to treat shared
x=  70.9 y= 115.3 x1= 230.9 sz=[10.9] ['URWPalladioL-Roma'] | layers as a special execution unit.
x=  87.8 y= 135.5 x1= 524.4 sz=[11.0] ['URWPalladioL-Bold', 'URWPalladioL-Roma'] | Pipeline payload extensions provide the intermediate representations and sparse routing
x=  70.9 y= 149.2 x1= 524.4 sz=[10.9] ['URWPalladioL-Roma'] | information required by downstream consumers when the source and consumer layers cross a
x=  70.5 y= 162.8 x1= 524.4 sz=[10.8] ['URWPalladioL-Roma'] | pipeline boundary. These states are incorporated into the existing point-to-point communication
x=  70.5 y= 176.2 x1= 524.4 sz=[11.0] ['URWPalladioL-Roma'] | path and partitioned consistently with context parallelism, avoiding unnecessary replication
x=  70.4 y= 189.8 x1= 319.3 sz=[10.9] ['URWPalladioL-Roma'] | while maintaining the corresponding gradient flow.
x=  87.8 y= 210.0 x1= 524.8 sz=[10.9] ['URWPalladioL-Bold', 'URWPalladioL-Roma'] | Micro-batch-level shared-state management tracks the states associated with concurrently
x=  70.9 y= 223.7 x1= 524.4 sz=[10.8] ['URWPalladioL-Roma'] | active pipeline micro-batches and coordinates their lifetimes across forward execution, activation
x=  70.9 y= 237.3 x1= 524.6 sz=[10.8] ['URWPalladioL-Roma'] | recomputation, and backward propagation. Shared states are retained until their final consumer
x=  70.9 y= 250.8 x1= 524.4 sz=[10.9] ['URWPalladioL-Roma'] | has completed and are then released promptly to limit additional memory overhead. The same
x=  70.9 y= 264.4 x1= 524.4 sz=[10.8] ['URWPalladioL-Roma'] | runtime abstraction also handles stage placement and source–consumer relationships, allowing
x=  70.9 y= 277.9 x1= 524.4 sz=[10.8] ['URWPalladioL-Roma'] | the attention implementation to access shared states without depending on the physical pipeline
x=  70.9 y= 291.4 x1= 104.4 sz=[10.9] ['URWPalladioL-Roma'] | layout.
x=  87.8 y= 311.7 x1= 524.4 sz=[11.0] ['URWPalladioL-Roma'] | Together with lightweight adaptations to the optimizer, checkpointing, warm-up, and
x=  70.9 y= 325.3 x1= 524.8 sz=[10.8] ['URWPalladioL-Roma'] | computation-graph tracing workflows, these mechanisms enable CSA2 to operate transparently
x=  70.9 y= 338.8 x1= 416.5 sz=[10.9] ['URWPalladioL-Roma'] | under the existing distributed training interface and pipeline schedules.
x=  70.9 y= 369.1 x1= 138.4 sz=[10.9] ['URWPalladioL-BoldItal'] | 3.1.3. Engram
x=  70.9 y= 391.7 x1= 524.4 sz=[11.0] ['URWPalladioL-Roma'] | Engram embedding tables are partitioned by row across dedicated process groups of engram
x=  70.5 y= 405.2 x1= 524.4 sz=[11.0] ['URWPalladioL-Roma'] | parallel size. The group size controls the trade-off between per-device memory usage and
x=  70.9 y= 418.8 x1= 524.4 sz=[11.0] ['URWPalladioL-Roma'] | the communication scope of embedding lookups. Optimizer states are further sharded across
x=  70.9 y= 432.3 x1= 524.4 sz=[11.0] ['URWPalladioL-Roma'] | replicas of each table partition. Engram lookup indices depend solely on the input token
x=  70.9 y= 446.0 x1= 524.4 sz=[10.8] ['URWPalladioL-Roma'] | sequence. Embedding prefetch is therefore initiated for the entire local batch before each pipeline
x=  70.9 y= 459.5 x1= 524.4 sz=[10.8] ['URWPalladioL-Roma'] | stage begins processing microbatches for the current training step, minimizing interference with
x=  70.5 y= 473.0 x1= 524.6 sz=[11.0] ['URWPalladioL-Roma'] | pipeline execution. Embedding gradients are buffered during backward and returned to their
x=  70.9 y= 486.5 x1= 524.4 sz=[11.0] ['URWPalladioL-Roma'] | owning ranks after the backbone backward pass. For efficient integration with multimodal
x=  70.9 y= 500.0 x1= 524.4 sz=[11.0] ['URWPalladioL-Roma'] | training, embedding prefetch and gradient transfers are scheduled to overlap with the vision
x=  70.9 y= 513.6 x1= 525.8 sz=[11.0] ['URWPalladioL-Roma'] | encoder’s forward and backward computation. Embeddings are stored and fetched in FP8,
x=  70.4 y= 527.1 x1= 524.6 sz=[11.0] ['URWPalladioL-Roma'] | with the retrieved values and scaling factors passed directly to the following GEMM. For
x=  70.9 y= 540.8 x1= 524.4 sz=[10.8] ['URWPalladioL-Roma'] | Engram table updates, Sinkhorn normalization maintains row and column scaling vectors across
x=  70.9 y= 554.2 x1= 524.4 sz=[11.0] ['URWPalladioL-Roma'] | iterations to avoid repeated writes of the full normalized matrix. Row normalization and the
x=  70.9 y= 567.9 x1= 524.8 sz=[10.8] ['URWPalladioL-Roma'] | accumulation of partial column statistics are fused into a single kernel to further reduce memory
x=  70.9 y= 581.3 x1= 524.4 sz=[11.0] ['URWPalladioL-Roma'] | traffic. During RL rollouts, Engram embedding tables remain resident in GPU memory. This
x=  70.5 y= 594.9 x1= 524.8 sz=[11.0] ['URWPalladioL-Roma'] | placement reduces host memory pressure and helps avoid out-of-memory failures caused by
x=  70.9 y= 608.5 x1= 209.1 sz=[10.9] ['URWPalladioL-Roma'] | host memory fragmentation.
x=  70.9 y= 641.5 x1= 179.0 sz=[10.9] ['URWPalladioL-Bold'] | 3.2. Inference System
x=  70.9 y= 663.9 x1= 524.4 sz=[10.9] ['URWPalladioL-Roma'] | DeepSeek-V4.1-Flash is designed with inference efficiency as a first-class concern. Although its
x=  70.9 y= 677.5 x1= 526.3 sz=[10.9] ['URWPalladioL-Roma'] | architecture is conceptually complex, the resulting inference kernel flow is remarkably concise.
x=  70.5 y= 691.0 x1= 524.4 sz=[10.9] ['URWPalladioL-Roma'] | Through reasonable kernel fusion, we encapsulate the intricate operations and keep hardware
x=  70.9 y= 704.5 x1= 526.2 sz=[11.0] ['URWPalladioL-Roma'] | resources fully pipelined inside a small number of fused kernels—including the fused-RoPE-
x=  70.9 y= 717.9 x1= 524.4 sz=[11.0] ['URWPalladioL-Ital', 'URWPalladioL-Roma'] | attention-RoPE-cast kernel in FlashMLA (Li and Liu, 2025), the Mega-Gate, Mega-mHC, and
x=  70.9 y= 731.6 x1= 525.8 sz=[11.0] ['URWPalladioL-Roma'] | Mega-MoE kernels in DeepGEMM (Zhao et al., 2025), the kernels in TileKernels (Wang et al.,
x=  70.9 y= 745.1 x1= 524.4 sz=[11.0] ['URWPalladioL-Roma'] | 2026a), and the TopK kernel in DeepSelect (Qian et al., 2026). As a result, the vast majority of
x= 292.2 y= 778.9 x1= 303.1 sz=[10.9] ['URWPalladioL-Roma'] | 18

========== PAGE 19 (draw=0) ==========
x=  70.5 y=  88.2 x1= 524.4 sz=[10.9] ['URWPalladioL-Roma'] | Transformer layers—those whose CSA2 operates in Reuse Mode—execute with only 15 kernels
x=  70.9 y= 101.7 x1= 524.8 sz=[10.9] ['URWPalladioL-Roma'] | during prefill and 11 during decode, thereby achieving both high-throughput and low-latency
x=  70.9 y= 115.3 x1= 117.7 sz=[10.9] ['URWPalladioL-Roma'] | inference.
x=  87.8 y= 135.6 x1= 524.4 sz=[10.9] ['URWPalladioL-Roma'] | At the deployment level, we adopt Encoder–Prefill–Decode (EPD) disaggregation, enabling
x=  70.6 y= 149.1 x1= 489.9 sz=[10.9] ['URWPalladioL-Roma'] | vision encoding, prefill, and decoding to scale independently and overlap in execution.
x=  70.9 y= 179.5 x1= 265.6 sz=[10.9] ['URWPalladioL-BoldItal'] | 3.2.1. Persistent KV Cache Management
x=  70.9 y= 202.0 x1= 526.3 sz=[10.9] ['URWPalladioL-Roma'] | Under identical workloads, V4.1 reduces the persistent KV cache footprint to 1/8 of that of V4.
x=  70.5 y= 215.5 x1= 524.4 sz=[10.9] ['URWPalladioL-Roma'] | Two multiplicative factors account for this reduction: the persistent KV cache no longer stores
x=  70.9 y= 229.2 x1= 524.4 sz=[10.9] ['URWPalladioL-Roma'] | SWA KV, which almost halves its size, and the global KV retained in it is further compressed to
x=  70.3 y= 242.7 x1= 414.7 sz=[10.9] ['URWPalladioL-Roma'] | 1/4 of V4’s footprint through architectural and precision optimizations.
x=  87.8 y= 263.0 x1= 526.3 sz=[10.9] ['URWPalladioL-Roma'] | In the V4 deployment, SWA KV accounts for nearly half of the persistent KV cache capacity.
x=  70.3 y= 276.5 x1= 524.4 sz=[11.0] ['URWPalladioL-Roma'] | Within this cache, global KV and SWA KV are managed independently, governed by an LRU
x=  70.9 y= 290.1 x1= 526.3 sz=[10.9] ['URWPalladioL-Roma'] | eviction policy. Global KV is stored in its entirety, and upon a hit, the complete prefix is reused.
x=  70.9 y= 303.6 x1= 524.4 sz=[11.0] ['URWPalladioL-Roma'] | In contrast, SWA KV is cached at two specific points—the end of the prompt and the end of
x=  70.9 y= 317.1 x1= 524.4 sz=[11.0] ['URWPalladioL-Roma'] | the output—to facilitate regeneration and multi-turn sessions; a hit allows computation to
x=  70.9 y= 330.7 x1= 524.8 sz=[11.0] ['URWPalladioL-Roma'] | resume from that cached position. To maintain a high hit rate, we configured a sufficiently
x=  70.9 y= 344.3 x1= 524.4 sz=[10.9] ['URWPalladioL-Roma'] | large persistent KV cache on SSD such that, under typical workloads, both types of KV remain
x=  70.9 y= 355.7 x1= 524.4 sz=[8.0, 10.0, 11.0] ['URWPalladioL-Roma', 'XCharterMathMI'] | resident for over 72 hours. Despite retaining only 𝑛win KV entries at designated positions, the
x=  70.9 y= 371.4 x1= 524.4 sz=[10.8, 10.9] ['URWPalladioL-Roma'] | uncompressed SWA KV cache still incurs a substantial storage overhead, especially in multi-turn
x=  70.9 y= 384.9 x1= 220.8 sz=[10.9] ['URWPalladioL-Roma'] | conversations with short turns.
x=  87.8 y= 405.2 x1= 524.4 sz=[11.0] ['URWPalladioL-Roma'] | Persistently storing SWA KV is both costly and ineffective, because its access pattern does
x=  70.9 y= 418.7 x1= 524.4 sz=[11.0] ['URWPalladioL-Roma'] | not match the persistent KV cache’s long retention policy. Unlike global KV, which exhibits
x=  70.9 y= 432.4 x1= 524.4 sz=[10.9] ['URWPalladioL-Roma'] | long-tail reuse, SWA KV is reused only within a narrow, minute-scale window inside an active
x=  70.9 y= 445.8 x1= 524.4 sz=[11.0] ['URWPalladioL-Roma'] | session and becomes dead once the session ends or the next turn begins. The V4 technical
x=  70.9 y= 459.3 x1= 524.4 sz=[10.8] ['URWPalladioL-Ital', 'URWPalladioL-Roma'] | report proposed Zero SWA Caching, which avoids the storage overhead by recomputing missing
x=  70.9 y= 470.9 x1= 524.4 sz=[8.0, 10.0, 10.8, 10.9] ['URWPalladioL-Roma', 'XCharterMathMI', 'txsys'] | SWA KV. Exact recovery, however, requires a full forward pass over 𝐿× 𝑛win tokens, whose cost
x=  70.5 y= 486.6 x1= 298.6 sz=[10.9] ['URWPalladioL-Roma'] | proved prohibitive in production deployments.
x=  87.8 y= 506.9 x1= 408.8 sz=[10.9] ['URWPalladioL-Roma'] | V4.1 therefore revises persistent KV cache management as follows:
x=  84.5 y= 539.2 x1= 524.4 sz=[10.8, 10.9] ['URWPalladioL-Roma'] | 1. SWA KV is no longer cached in the persistent KV cache and instead stored in a distributed
x=  98.1 y= 552.7 x1= 524.4 sz=[11.0] ['URWPalladioL-Roma'] | memory pool provisioned from 10% of the host DRAM on each machine. Although this
x=  97.8 y= 566.2 x1= 524.4 sz=[11.0] ['URWPalladioL-Roma'] | pool is far smaller in aggregate capacity, its short TTL (only minutes) allows expired
x=  98.1 y= 579.7 x1= 524.4 sz=[11.0] ['URWPalladioL-Roma'] | entries to be recycled immediately for new sessions; under real-world workloads, this
x=  98.1 y= 593.4 x1= 524.8 sz=[10.9] ['URWPalladioL-Roma'] | high turnover suffices to serve the vast majority of concurrent active sessions. Global KV
x=  98.1 y= 606.9 x1= 490.7 sz=[10.9] ['URWPalladioL-Roma'] | remains in the persistent KV cache with a guaranteed lifetime of at least 72 hours.
x=  84.5 y= 620.5 x1= 524.4 sz=[10.9] ['URWPalladioL-Roma'] | 2. Evicting SWA KV inevitably causes misses, which stay affordable thanks to a lightweight
x=  98.1 y= 633.7 x1= 524.4 sz=[11.0] ['URWPalladioL-Ital', 'URWPalladioL-Roma'] | fallback, Encoder SWA Bounded Replay (detailed in Section 3.2.2). For the inevitable but
x=  98.1 y= 647.5 x1= 524.4 sz=[11.0] ['URWPalladioL-Roma'] | infrequent requests that hit global KV but miss SWA KV, it recovers the missing state
x=  98.1 y= 659.0 x1= 524.4 sz=[8.0, 10.0, 10.9, 11.0] ['URWPalladioL-Roma', 'XCharterMathMI', 'txsys'] | by recomputing only 𝑛win tokens instead of a full 𝐿× 𝑛win-token forward pass. This
x=  98.1 y= 674.7 x1= 525.8 sz=[10.8] ['URWPalladioL-Roma'] | bounded replay is the cornerstone of the design: it turns a catastrophic miss into a graceful,
x=  98.1 y= 688.1 x1= 524.4 sz=[11.0] ['URWPalladioL-Roma'] | inexpensive degradation, thereby justifying the removal of SWA KV from the persistent
x=  98.1 y= 701.7 x1= 146.1 sz=[10.9] ['URWPalladioL-Roma'] | KV cache.
x= 292.2 y= 778.9 x1= 303.1 sz=[10.9] ['URWPalladioL-Roma'] | 19

========== PAGE 20 (draw=2) ==========
x=  70.9 y=  87.9 x1= 207.4 sz=[10.9] ['URWPalladioL-BoldItal'] | 3.2.2. SWA Bounded Replay
x=  70.9 y= 108.4 x1= 524.4 sz=[10.0, 10.8] ['URWPalladioL-Roma', 'XCharterMathMI'] | Since SWA dependencies accumulate across layers, exactly reconstructing the SWA KV of 𝐿layers
x=  70.4 y= 121.9 x1= 524.4 sz=[8.0, 10.0, 10.9, 11.0] ['URWPalladioL-Roma', 'XCharterMathMI', 'txsys'] | would require replaying 𝐿× 𝑛win tokens. SWA Bounded Replay instead replays only the most
x=  70.9 y= 135.5 x1= 524.4 sz=[8.0, 10.0, 10.8] ['URWPalladioL-Roma', 'XCharterMathMI'] | recent 𝑛win tokens and truncates SWA to the replay segment, accepting approximate states: for a
x=  70.9 y= 149.0 x1= 417.7 sz=[10.0, 10.9] ['URWPalladioL-Roma', 'XCharterMathMI'] | replay starting at position 𝑠, a query at position 𝑖attends to SWA keys in
x= 425.3 y= 149.0 x1= 515.1 sz=[10.0, 10.9] ['URWPalladioL-Roma', 'XCharterMathMI', 'txsys'] | max(𝑠, 𝑖−𝑊+ 1), 𝑖
x= 421.4 y= 149.2 x1= 425.1 sz=[10.9] ['txexs'] | 
x= 515.4 y= 149.2 x1= 519.0 sz=[10.9] ['txexs'] | 
x= 520.0 y= 151.1 x1= 522.8 sz=[10.9] ['URWPalladioL-Roma'] | .
x=  70.9 y= 186.7 x1= 224.4 sz=[10.8] ['URWPalladioL-Bold'] | Encoder SWA Bounded Replay.
x= 235.3 y= 186.8 x1= 524.4 sz=[10.8] ['URWPalladioL-Roma'] | Encoder SWA Bounded Replay makes prefix caching depend
x=  70.9 y= 200.3 x1= 466.9 sz=[10.9] ['URWPalladioL-Roma'] | only on global KV, allowing SWA KV to be removed from the persistent KV cache.
x=  87.8 y= 218.5 x1= 524.7 sz=[8.0, 10.0, 11.0] ['URWPalladioL-Roma', 'XCharterMathMI'] | When the encoder SWA KV is missing, we replay the last 𝑛win tokens of the cached prefix
x=  70.9 y= 234.2 x1= 524.8 sz=[10.9] ['URWPalladioL-Roma'] | and process them together with the uncached suffix. The replayed tokens regenerate only SWA
x=  70.9 y= 247.6 x1= 524.4 sz=[11.0] ['URWPalladioL-Roma'] | KV, reusing the cached global KV without recomputation or overwriting, while the uncached
x=  70.9 y= 261.2 x1= 290.1 sz=[10.9] ['URWPalladioL-Roma'] | suffix generates both global KV and SWA KV.
x=  87.8 y= 281.6 x1= 524.4 sz=[10.8] ['URWPalladioL-Roma'] | By design, the replayed prefix state is approximate, so the global KV and SWA KV computed
x=  70.9 y= 295.1 x1= 524.4 sz=[11.0] ['URWPalladioL-Roma'] | for the uncached suffix depend on the cache-hit position and are not mathematically identical
x=  70.9 y= 308.7 x1= 524.8 sz=[10.9] ['URWPalladioL-Roma'] | across positions. Encouragingly, our experimental evidence confirms that this bounded replay
x=  70.9 y= 322.2 x1= 293.3 sz=[10.9] ['URWPalladioL-Roma'] | strategy barely compromises response quality.
x=  70.9 y= 357.8 x1= 226.2 sz=[10.8] ['URWPalladioL-Bold'] | Decoder SWA Bounded Replay.
x= 237.0 y= 357.9 x1= 524.4 sz=[10.8] ['URWPalladioL-Roma'] | Decoder SWA Bounded Replay bounds the decoder forward
x=  70.5 y= 369.3 x1= 359.6 sz=[8.0, 10.0, 10.9] ['URWPalladioL-Roma', 'XCharterMathMI'] | pass to 𝑛win tokens, nearly halving total prefill computation.
x=  87.8 y= 391.7 x1= 524.8 sz=[10.9] ['URWPalladioL-Roma'] | Under CED, decoder global KV is projected from the final encoder hidden states. The only
x=  70.9 y= 405.1 x1= 524.4 sz=[11.0] ['URWPalladioL-Roma'] | obstacle to ending prefill at the encoder is decoder SWA KV, which is generated from each
x=  70.9 y= 418.9 x1= 524.4 sz=[10.8] ['URWPalladioL-Roma'] | decoder layer’s own hidden states and is needed by the first decode steps. Since we never cache
x=  70.9 y= 428.6 x1= 387.0 sz=[7.3, 11.0] ['URWPalladioL-Roma', 'XCharterMathMI'] | decoder SWA KV, exactly reconstructing it requires running the 𝐿
x= 383.1 y= 432.3 x1= 524.4 sz=[8.0, 11.0] ['URWPalladioL-Roma'] | 2 decoder layers over the last
x=  72.4 y= 442.2 x1=  76.0 sz=[7.3] ['XCharterMathMI'] | 𝐿
x=  72.1 y= 443.8 x1= 524.4 sz=[8.0, 10.0, 10.8, 10.9] ['URWPalladioL-Roma', 'XCharterMathMI', 'txsys'] | 2 × 𝑛win prompt tokens, which is expensive when a short uncached suffix follows a long cached
x=  70.5 y= 459.5 x1= 524.4 sz=[10.8] ['URWPalladioL-Roma'] | prefix. Therefore, we also apply the bounded replay strategy to this scenario: at every prefill, we
x=  70.9 y= 470.9 x1= 524.4 sz=[8.0, 10.0, 10.8] ['URWPalladioL-Roma', 'XCharterMathMI'] | replay the last 𝑛win tokens of the prompt, feed their encoder outputs through the decoder layers
x=  70.9 y= 486.6 x1= 524.4 sz=[10.9] ['URWPalladioL-Roma'] | under the same SWA truncation, and use the resulting decoder SWA KV only for decoding, not
x=  70.9 y= 500.1 x1= 158.1 sz=[10.9] ['URWPalladioL-Roma'] | for prefix caching.
x=  87.8 y= 520.3 x1= 524.4 sz=[11.0] ['URWPalladioL-Roma'] | By design, the reconstructed decoder SWA KV is not mathematically equivalent to that
x=  70.9 y= 533.9 x1= 524.4 sz=[11.0] ['URWPalladioL-Roma'] | from a full decoder forward pass. Also, we find that this strategy has only a negligible impact
x=  70.9 y= 547.4 x1= 526.2 sz=[11.0] ['URWPalladioL-Roma'] | on response quality. For added safety, we additionally simulate the same replay during post-
x=  70.9 y= 561.0 x1= 242.1 sz=[10.9] ['URWPalladioL-Roma'] | training for train-aware adaptation.

x=  70.9 y= 672.9 x1= 524.4 sz=[11.0] ['URWPalladioL-Roma'] | among diverse corpora that offer unique information gains. We adopt a more systematic and

x=  70.9 y= 700.1 x1= 524.4 sz=[10.8] ['URWPalladioL-Roma'] | Specifically, based on more comprehensive evaluations, a scaling ladder over model parameters
x=  70.9 y= 713.5 x1= 526.2 sz=[11.0] ['URWPalladioL-Roma'] | and training data is carefully designed to guide large-scale training runs. We filter out model-
x=  70.9 y= 727.1 x1= 524.4 sz=[11.0] ['URWPalladioL-Roma'] | generated content with limited information gain, including outputs from less capable models
x=  70.9 y= 740.6 x1= 524.4 sz=[11.0] ['URWPalladioL-Roma'] | and low-quality machine-translated text. We regard such content as implicit duplication, as
x= 292.2 y= 778.9 x1= 303.1 sz=[10.9] ['URWPalladioL-Roma'] | 20

========== PAGE 21 (draw=0) ==========
x=  70.9 y=  88.1 x1= 524.4 sz=[11.0] ['URWPalladioL-Roma'] | it largely reformulates existing information and may become detrimental over long training
x=  70.9 y= 101.6 x1= 524.6 sz=[11.0] ['URWPalladioL-Roma'] | horizons. We also explore model-in-the-loop data iteration approaches as a foundation for
x=  70.9 y= 115.2 x1= 524.4 sz=[11.0] ['URWPalladioL-Roma'] | future large-scale synthetic data. In addition, we involve more domain experts to construct

x=  70.9 y= 204.2 x1= 205.7 sz=[11.0] ['URWPalladioL-Bold'] | Multimodal Data Curation

x=  70.9 y=  88.1 x1= 524.4 sz=[11.0] ['URWPalladioL-Roma'] | decoder. For the first two layers, we use pure sliding window attention. The remaining 18

x=  70.9 y= 155.8 x1= 524.4 sz=[11.0] ['URWPalladioL-Roma'] | group, the first layer operates in Full Mode, and the remaining three layers operate in Reuse
x=  70.9 y= 169.4 x1= 524.4 sz=[11.0] ['URWPalladioL-Roma'] | Mode. The remaining four groups share the same configuration: the first layer operates in
x=  70.9 y= 183.0 x1= 524.4 sz=[10.9] ['URWPalladioL-Roma'] | Reindex Mode, and the remaining three layers operate in Reuse Mode. For all CSA2 layers, we
x=  70.9 y= 196.6 x1= 524.6 sz=[10.8] ['URWPalladioL-Roma'] | set the number of indexer query heads to 32, the indexer head dimension to 128, and the number
x=  70.9 y= 210.0 x1= 524.4 sz=[11.0] ['URWPalladioL-Roma'] | of KV entries selected for sparse attention (i.e., attention top-k) to 512. We set the number of
x=  70.6 y= 223.6 x1= 526.3 sz=[11.0] ['URWPalladioL-Roma'] | query heads to 64, the head dimension to 512, and the query compression dimension to 1280.
x=  70.9 y= 237.1 x1= 525.8 sz=[11.0] ['URWPalladioL-Roma'] | For the Hierarchical Sparse Indexer, we select a maximum of 2,048 blocks with 8 positions,
x=  70.6 y= 250.8 x1= 524.4 sz=[10.8] ['URWPalladioL-Roma'] | yielding up to 16,384 candidate positions in total. The number of output projection groups is set
x=  70.9 y= 264.3 x1= 524.4 sz=[10.9] ['URWPalladioL-Roma'] | to 8, and the dimension of each intermediate attention output is set to 1024. For the additional
x=  70.9 y= 275.8 x1= 524.4 sz=[8.0, 10.0, 10.9] ['URWPalladioL-Roma', 'XCharterMathMI'] | branch of sliding window attention, the window size 𝑛win is set to 128. We employ MoE layers
x=  70.9 y= 291.5 x1= 524.4 sz=[10.8] ['URWPalladioL-Roma'] | in all Transformer blocks, using SwiGLU activation function with clamping (OpenAI, 2025) at a
x=  70.9 y= 304.9 x1= 524.4 sz=[11.0] ['URWPalladioL-Roma'] | threshold of 10. Each MoE layer consists of 1 shared expert and 384 routed experts, where the
x=  70.9 y= 318.4 x1= 524.4 sz=[11.0] ['URWPalladioL-Roma'] | intermediate hidden dimension of each expert is 2304. Among the routed experts, 6 experts
x=  70.4 y= 331.8 x1= 524.6 sz=[11.0] ['URWPalladioL-Ital', 'URWPalladioL-Roma'] | will be activated for each token. As for mHC, the expansion factor is set to 4, and the number
x=  70.9 y= 345.5 x1= 524.4 sz=[11.0] ['URWPalladioL-Roma'] | of Sinkhorn-Knopp iterations is set to 20. For the vision encoder, we set its number of layers
x=  70.9 y= 359.1 x1= 524.4 sz=[10.9] ['URWPalladioL-Roma'] | to 32, the hidden dimension to 1024, the number of attention heads to 16, and the image patch
x=  70.9 y= 372.7 x1= 524.4 sz=[11.0] ['URWPalladioL-Roma'] | size to 14. The vision MLP projector has 2 layers with a hidden dimension of 5120. Under this
x=  70.9 y= 386.2 x1= 524.4 sz=[11.0] ['URWPalladioL-Roma'] | configuration, DeepSeek-V4.1-Flash comprises 552B backbone parameters, with 8B activated
x=  70.5 y= 399.8 x1= 301.4 sz=[10.9] ['URWPalladioL-Roma'] | per token during prefill and 16B during decode.
x=  70.9 y= 430.1 x1= 176.5 sz=[10.9] ['URWPalladioL-BoldItal'] | 4.2.2. Training Setups
x=  70.3 y= 452.7 x1= 524.6 sz=[10.9] ['URWPalladioL-Roma'] | We employ the Muon optimizer (Jordan et al., 2024; Liu et al., 2025) for the parameters of linear
x=  70.9 y= 466.2 x1= 524.4 sz=[11.0] ['URWPalladioL-Roma'] | transformations, use AdamW optimizer (Loshchilov and Hutter, 2019) for the weights of all
x=  70.9 y= 479.9 x1= 524.4 sz=[10.8] ['URWPalladioL-Roma'] | RMSNorm modules and other non-matrix parameters, and use Sinkhorn-balanced update for all
x=  70.9 y= 491.2 x1= 525.8 sz=[8.0, 10.0, 10.8, 10.9] ['URWPalladioL-Roma', 'XCharterMathMI', 'txmiaX'] | embeddings and prediction head. For AdamW, we set its hyper-parameters to 𝛽1 = 0.9, 𝛽2 = 0.95,
x=  70.9 y= 504.2 x1= 524.4 sz=[8.0, 10.0, 10.9, 11.0] ['URWPalladioL-Roma', 'XCharterMathMI', 'txmiaX', 'txsys'] | 𝜀= 10−20, and weight_decay = 0.1. For Muon, we set the momentum to 0.95 and the weight
x=  70.9 y= 520.4 x1= 524.9 sz=[10.9] ['URWPalladioL-Roma'] | decay to 0.1, and rescale the RMS of each update matrix to 0.18 for reutilization of the AdamW
x=  70.9 y= 533.9 x1= 524.4 sz=[11.0] ['URWPalladioL-Roma'] | learning rate. For Sinkhorn-balanced updating, we use the same momentum coefficient and
x=  70.9 y= 544.8 x1= 524.4 sz=[8.0, 10.0, 10.8, 10.9] ['URWPalladioL-Roma', 'XCharterMathMI', 'txmiaX', 'txsys'] | learning-rate correction factor as for Muon and set 𝐾= 11, 𝜏= 10−3, 𝜀= 10−20. Following (Cheng
x=  70.9 y= 559.9 x1= 524.4 sz=[10.9, 11.0] ['URWPalladioL-Roma', 'txsys'] | et al., 2026b), the learning rate of Engram is scaled by 5×. We train DeepSeek-V4.1-Flash on
x=  70.9 y= 574.6 x1= 524.4 sz=[10.9] ['URWPalladioL-Roma'] | 45T tokens of multimodal data with no instability. We keep the batch size fixed at 100.6 million
x=  70.9 y= 588.1 x1= 524.4 sz=[11.0] ['URWPalladioL-Roma'] | tokens throughout training. The learning rate is linearly warmed up over the first 2000 steps
x=  70.9 y= 597.8 x1= 524.4 sz=[8.0, 10.9] ['URWPalladioL-Roma', 'txsys'] | and then maintained at 2.6 × 10−4 until 28T tokens. Between 28T and 40T tokens, we decay the
x=  70.9 y= 611.3 x1= 524.4 sz=[8.0, 10.9, 11.0] ['URWPalladioL-Roma', 'txsys'] | learning rate to 2.6 × 10−5 following a cosine schedule. We keep the learning rate at this value
x=  70.9 y= 628.7 x1= 524.4 sz=[11.0] ['URWPalladioL-Roma'] | from 40T to 45T tokens. We train the model from scratch with sparse attention at a sequence
x=  70.9 y= 642.4 x1= 524.4 sz=[10.9] ['URWPalladioL-Roma'] | length of 64K and extend the sequence length to 1M at 34T tokens. For auxiliary-loss-free load
x=  70.9 y= 656.0 x1= 524.4 sz=[10.8] ['URWPalladioL-Roma'] | balancing, we set the bias update speed to 0.001 for both image and text tokens, while retaining
x=  70.9 y= 669.4 x1= 524.4 sz=[11.0] ['URWPalladioL-Roma'] | a small sequence-level balance loss with a loss weight of 0.0001 to avoid extreme imbalance
x=  70.4 y= 683.0 x1= 524.4 sz=[11.0] ['URWPalladioL-Roma'] | within single sequences. Similar to DeepSeek-V4, we employ sample-level attention masking
x=  70.9 y= 696.6 x1= 167.4 sz=[10.9] ['URWPalladioL-Roma'] | during pre-training.
x=  70.9 y= 732.1 x1= 192.5 sz=[10.8] ['URWPalladioL-Bold'] | Vision Encoder Training.
x= 203.4 y= 732.2 x1= 524.4 sz=[10.8] ['URWPalladioL-Roma'] | Our DeepSeek-ViT undergoes a separate training stage before being
x=  70.9 y= 745.5 x1= 524.4 sz=[10.9] ['URWPalladioL-Ital', 'URWPalladioL-Roma'] | integrated with the language backbone. The training pipeline consists of two stages: contrastive
x= 292.2 y= 778.9 x1= 303.1 sz=[10.9] ['URWPalladioL-Roma'] | 22

========== PAGE 23 (draw=0) ==========
x=  70.9 y=  88.1 x1= 524.4 sz=[10.8] ['URWPalladioL-Ital', 'URWPalladioL-Roma'] | pre-training and autoregressive fine-tuning. During contrastive pretraining, we optimize the model
x=  70.9 y= 101.8 x1= 524.4 sz=[10.8] ['URWPalladioL-Roma'] | using the sigmoid contrastive loss introduced by SigLIP (Zhai et al., 2023) on approximately 47B
x=  70.9 y= 115.3 x1= 524.4 sz=[10.8] ['URWPalladioL-Roma'] | image-text pairs sourced from alt-text data. To efficiently learn visual representations from such
x=  70.9 y= 127.7 x1= 524.4 sz=[10.9] ['URWPalladioL-Roma', 'txsys'] | massive datasets, we restrict the maximum input resolution to 224 × 224 pixels by downscaling
x=  70.9 y= 142.3 x1= 524.4 sz=[11.0] ['URWPalladioL-Roma'] | larger images while preserving their aspect ratios. Although using higher resolutions in this
x=  70.5 y= 155.8 x1= 524.4 sz=[11.0] ['URWPalladioL-Roma'] | phase yields notable gains, empirical results show that these benefits contribute little to the
x=  70.9 y= 169.4 x1= 524.4 sz=[11.0] ['URWPalladioL-Roma'] | final model. Because the subsequent autoregressive stage specifically handles high-resolution
x=  70.9 y= 182.9 x1= 524.4 sz=[11.0] ['URWPalladioL-Roma'] | extrapolation, scaling up resolutions during contrastive pretraining significantly increases
x=  70.9 y= 196.6 x1= 524.4 sz=[10.9] ['URWPalladioL-Roma'] | computational overhead without much overall improvement. In the autoregressive fine-tuning
x=  70.9 y= 210.2 x1= 524.4 sz=[10.8] ['URWPalladioL-Roma'] | stage, we connect the vision encoder to a 4B MoE LLM and train on 236B tokens across datasets
x=  70.9 y= 223.7 x1= 524.4 sz=[10.8] ['URWPalladioL-Roma'] | including image captions, alt text, charts, and OCR, using a next-token prediction objective. This
x=  70.9 y= 237.2 x1= 524.4 sz=[10.9] ['URWPalladioL-Roma'] | stage aims to enhance the encoder’s ability to model fine-grained visual features. We therefore
x=  70.9 y= 249.6 x1= 524.4 sz=[10.8, 10.9] ['URWPalladioL-Roma', 'txsys'] | constrain the input resolution between 544 × 544 and 1344 × 1344 pixels by proportionally scaling
x=  70.9 y= 264.4 x1= 524.4 sz=[10.8] ['URWPalladioL-Roma'] | out-of-bound images. After this stage, we discard the LLM and retain only the optimized vision
x=  70.9 y= 277.8 x1= 524.4 sz=[11.0] ['URWPalladioL-Roma'] | encoder for the subsequent pre-training pipeline, where the same input-resolution policy is
x=  70.9 y= 291.4 x1= 128.6 sz=[10.9] ['URWPalladioL-Roma'] | maintained.

x=  70.9 y= 249.9 x1= 524.4 sz=[10.9] ['URWPalladioL-Roma', 'txsys'] | Figure 6 | Bits-per-bytes (BPB) comparison of DeepSeek-V4-Flash-Base, DeepSeek-V4-Pro-Base

x=  70.9 y= 325.1 x1= 166.9 sz=[13.0] ['URWPalladioL-Bold'] | 5. Post-Training
x=  70.9 y= 353.0 x1= 204.5 sz=[10.9] ['URWPalladioL-Bold'] | 5.1. Post-Training Pipeline
x=  70.9 y= 375.4 x1= 524.4 sz=[11.0] ['URWPalladioL-Roma'] | In this release, we refrain from introducing novel post-training algorithms. The overall recipe
x=  70.9 y= 389.0 x1= 526.2 sz=[10.8] ['URWPalladioL-Roma'] | follows the standard paradigm of supervised fine-tuning (SFT) followed by reinforcement learn-
x=  70.9 y= 402.6 x1= 524.4 sz=[10.8] ['URWPalladioL-Roma'] | ing (RL) and on-policy distillation (OPD; Gu et al., 2024; Lu and Lab, 2025), without algorithmic
x=  70.9 y= 416.0 x1= 524.4 sz=[11.0] ['URWPalladioL-Roma'] | modifications beyond well-established practices. Instead, our efforts are concentrated almost
x=  70.9 y= 429.5 x1= 526.2 sz=[10.9, 11.0] ['URWPalladioL-Roma'] | entirely on what the model is trained on rather than how it is optimized: we invest in large-
x=  70.9 y= 443.1 x1= 524.4 sz=[11.0] ['URWPalladioL-Roma'] | scale, automated pipelines for data synthesis and environment construction. Concretely, the
x=  70.5 y= 456.7 x1= 524.4 sz=[10.9] ['URWPalladioL-Roma'] | pipeline (i) synthesizes diverse, verifiable training tasks together with their reference solutions
x=  70.9 y= 470.2 x1= 524.4 sz=[11.0] ['URWPalladioL-Roma'] | and reward signals, (ii) procedurally constructs and scales interactive agent environments in
x=  70.4 y= 483.8 x1= 525.8 sz=[10.9] ['URWPalladioL-Roma'] | which trajectories can be collected and evaluated at low cost, and (iii) applies rigorous filtering,
x=  70.9 y= 497.4 x1= 524.4 sz=[10.8] ['URWPalladioL-Roma'] | deduplication, and difficulty calibration to ensure data quality and curriculum balance. We find
x=  70.9 y= 510.9 x1= 524.4 sz=[10.9] ['URWPalladioL-Roma'] | that, under a fixed and unremarkable optimization procedure, systematic improvements in the
x=  70.9 y= 524.4 x1= 524.8 sz=[11.0] ['URWPalladioL-Roma'] | scale, diversity, and verifiability of synthesized data and environments account for essentially
x=  70.9 y= 537.9 x1= 524.4 sz=[11.0] ['URWPalladioL-Roma'] | all of the observed gains. This observation echoes a broader lesson: at the current stage, the
x=  70.9 y= 551.6 x1= 524.4 sz=[10.9] ['URWPalladioL-Roma'] | marginal return of engineering the data and environment pipeline substantially exceeds that of
x=  70.9 y= 565.1 x1= 246.2 sz=[10.9] ['URWPalladioL-Roma'] | algorithmic novelty in post-training.
x=  70.9 y= 595.4 x1= 263.4 sz=[10.9] ['URWPalladioL-BoldItal'] | 5.1.1. Large-Scale Agent Task Synthesis
x=  70.5 y= 617.9 x1= 524.8 sz=[11.0] ['URWPalladioL-Roma'] | Tasks serve as the fundamental fuel for agent learning. However, constructing high-quality
x=  70.9 y= 631.6 x1= 524.4 sz=[10.8] ['URWPalladioL-Roma'] | training tasks has traditionally required substantial manual effort. We observe that the model is
x=  70.9 y= 645.1 x1= 524.8 sz=[10.8] ['URWPalladioL-Roma'] | already beginning to exhibit the ability to construct its own training tasks, though this capability
x=  70.9 y= 658.5 x1= 524.4 sz=[11.0] ['URWPalladioL-Roma'] | remains far from perfect. Recognizing this potential, we have invested considerable effort in
x=  70.9 y= 672.2 x1= 442.4 sz=[10.9] ['URWPalladioL-Roma'] | strengthening the model’s task-construction and quality-verification abilities.
x=  87.8 y= 692.5 x1= 524.4 sz=[10.8] ['URWPalladioL-Roma'] | We formalize each task as a triplet (problem, environment, verification system) and evaluate
x=  70.9 y= 705.9 x1= 526.2 sz=[11.0] ['URWPalladioL-Roma'] | its quality along two dimensions: difficulty—ensuring the task is non-trivial—and correct-
x=  70.9 y= 719.5 x1= 524.8 sz=[11.0] ['URWPalladioL-Roma'] | ness—guaranteeing that no critical flaws exist among the three components. Using difficulty
x=  70.9 y= 733.1 x1= 524.4 sz=[11.0] ['URWPalladioL-Roma'] | and correctness as reward signals, we iteratively train the model to construct better tasks. We
x=  70.9 y= 746.6 x1= 525.8 sz=[11.0] ['URWPalladioL-Roma'] | also monitor RL tasks across their full lifecycle. Whenever a task is used in a new RL run,
x= 292.2 y= 778.9 x1= 303.1 sz=[10.9] ['URWPalladioL-Roma'] | 25

========== PAGE 26 (draw=0) ==========
x=  70.9 y=  88.1 x1= 524.4 sz=[11.0] ['URWPalladioL-Roma'] | the resulting trajectories provide fresh evidence for quality re-auditing. Under this general
x=  70.9 y= 101.6 x1= 524.4 sz=[11.0] ['URWPalladioL-Roma'] | framework, we have built dedicated training environment production pipelines for two core
x=  70.9 y= 115.3 x1= 285.1 sz=[10.9] ['URWPalladioL-Roma'] | scenarios: general agents and coding agents.
x=  87.8 y= 135.5 x1= 524.4 sz=[10.8] ['URWPalladioL-Bold', 'URWPalladioL-Roma'] | General Agent. For general agents, we encourage internal employees and external partners
x=  70.9 y= 149.1 x1= 524.4 sz=[11.0] ['URWPalladioL-Roma'] | to incorporate our latest model into their routine workflows and, on a voluntary basis, return
x=  70.9 y= 162.6 x1= 524.4 sz=[11.0] ['URWPalladioL-Roma'] | interaction data and feedback. Based on the interfaces observed in the returned data, we
x=  70.9 y= 176.2 x1= 524.4 sz=[10.9] ['URWPalladioL-Roma'] | construct a large set of mocked tools that reproduce the interfaces and behaviors of real-world
x=  70.9 y= 189.9 x1= 524.4 sz=[10.8] ['URWPalladioL-Roma'] | tools and systems, including their input formats, output structures, API schemas, and behavioral
x=  70.9 y= 203.3 x1= 524.4 sz=[11.0] ['URWPalladioL-Roma'] | constraints, covering both commonly used SaaS and enterprise applications as well as more
x=  70.9 y= 216.8 x1= 524.4 sz=[11.0] ['URWPalladioL-Roma'] | specialized business back-end systems. In parallel, we collect negative feedback and model
x=  70.9 y= 230.4 x1= 524.4 sz=[11.0] ['URWPalladioL-Roma'] | failure cases submitted by internal employees at scale, and incorporate them into the pipeline
x=  70.9 y= 243.9 x1= 526.3 sz=[11.0] ['URWPalladioL-Roma'] | to generate both single-turn and multi-turn agent environments grounded in real workflows.
x=  70.9 y= 257.5 x1= 525.8 sz=[11.0] ['URWPalladioL-Roma'] | By reconstructing the relevant tool context, user interaction patterns, and failure conditions,
x=  70.9 y= 271.0 x1= 524.4 sz=[11.0] ['URWPalladioL-Roma'] | the pipeline enables systematic replay of failures and targeted reinforcement learning against
x=  70.9 y= 284.6 x1= 210.4 sz=[10.9] ['URWPalladioL-Roma'] | observed model weaknesses.
x=  87.8 y= 304.8 x1= 526.2 sz=[10.9] ['URWPalladioL-Bold', 'URWPalladioL-Roma'] | Coding Agent. Coding agent training environments are built from two sources: 1) coding-
x=  70.9 y= 318.5 x1= 524.7 sz=[10.9] ['URWPalladioL-Roma'] | agent sessions from internal employees and external partners, filtered to retain highly complex
x=  70.9 y= 332.0 x1= 525.5 sz=[11.0] ['URWPalladioL-Roma'] | tasks or tasks on which model performance is poor, then deduplicated by trajectory; and 2)
x=  70.5 y= 345.7 x1= 524.4 sz=[10.8] ['URWPalladioL-Roma'] | public GitHub repositories that meet a star-count threshold. Environment construction is carried
x=  70.9 y= 359.2 x1= 524.4 sz=[10.8] ['URWPalladioL-Roma'] | out collaboratively by multiple specialized agents. First, an agent determines whether the project
x=  70.9 y= 372.8 x1= 524.4 sz=[10.8] ['URWPalladioL-Roma'] | can be built and fully run inside a container and whether it can be automatically verified; if so, it
x=  70.9 y= 386.3 x1= 524.7 sz=[10.9] ['URWPalladioL-Roma'] | selects a specific turn or commit as the task starting point, designs several sufficiently complex
x=  70.9 y= 399.9 x1= 524.4 sz=[10.8] ['URWPalladioL-Roma'] | implementation directions, and produces concrete evaluation points, including both fail-to-pass
x=  70.9 y= 413.3 x1= 524.4 sz=[10.9] ['URWPalladioL-Roma'] | and pass-to-pass points, along with a construction report, fetching external resources from the
x=  70.4 y= 426.9 x1= 524.4 sz=[10.9] ['URWPalladioL-Roma'] | web as needed. Next, a separate agent sets up dependencies, the initial working directory, test
x=  70.9 y= 440.4 x1= 524.4 sz=[11.0] ['URWPalladioL-Roma'] | code, and task descriptions in an isolated container, performs self-testing, removes any traces
x=  70.9 y= 453.9 x1= 525.8 sz=[11.0] ['URWPalladioL-Roma'] | that could leak the task solution, and packages the environment as a new image layer. Then,
x=  70.9 y= 467.6 x1= 524.4 sz=[10.9] ['URWPalladioL-Roma'] | multiple distinct agents attempt the task, and an independent quality-inspection agent reviews
x=  70.9 y= 481.2 x1= 525.8 sz=[10.8] ['URWPalladioL-Roma'] | the environment together with the solving agents’ trajectories, checking for environment issues,
x=  70.9 y= 494.6 x1= 524.8 sz=[11.0] ['URWPalladioL-Roma'] | factual errors, mismatches between evaluation points and task descriptions, and hackability
x=  70.9 y= 508.3 x1= 524.4 sz=[10.8] ['URWPalladioL-Roma'] | risks. If the inspection does not pass, a repair agent fixes all identified errors, adjusts evaluation
x=  70.5 y= 521.7 x1= 423.6 sz=[10.9] ['URWPalladioL-Roma'] | points that are too easy or too difficult, and the task re-enters verification.
x=  87.8 y= 542.0 x1= 524.4 sz=[11.0] ['URWPalladioL-Roma'] | Through these pipelines, we can automatically and batch-produce RL training data that is
x=  70.9 y= 555.6 x1= 524.4 sz=[11.0] ['URWPalladioL-Roma'] | correct, discriminative, and controllable in length and difficulty. Whether through the faithful
x=  70.9 y= 569.1 x1= 524.4 sz=[11.0] ['URWPalladioL-Roma'] | reconstruction of real workflows in general agent environments or the precise construction of
x=  70.9 y= 582.7 x1= 525.8 sz=[10.9] ['URWPalladioL-Roma'] | coding tasks in coding agent environments, both ultimately feed into a unified training system,
x=  70.9 y= 596.3 x1= 505.9 sz=[10.9] ['URWPalladioL-Roma'] | driving iterative improvement of model capabilities under continuous quality monitoring.
x=  70.9 y= 626.6 x1= 218.7 sz=[10.9] ['URWPalladioL-BoldItal'] | 5.1.2. RL in Synthesized Tasks
x=  70.3 y= 649.1 x1= 524.4 sz=[11.0] ['URWPalladioL-Roma'] | We use large-scale asynchronous RL in synthesized tasks to improve model performance and
x=  70.9 y= 662.7 x1= 524.4 sz=[10.9] ['URWPalladioL-Roma'] | shape its behavior in complicated scenarios. We scale RL runs in two dimensions, i.e., training
x=  70.9 y= 676.3 x1= 524.4 sz=[10.8] ['URWPalladioL-Roma'] | compute and the number of scaffolds. As shown in Figure 7 and Figure 8, performance continues
x=  70.9 y= 689.8 x1= 524.4 sz=[10.9] ['URWPalladioL-Roma'] | to improve as we increase compute with cumulative RL steps, whether scaling within a single
x=  70.9 y= 703.3 x1= 486.6 sz=[10.9] ['URWPalladioL-Roma'] | scaffold, jointly across variants of the same scaffold, or across heterogeneous scaffolds.
x=  87.8 y= 723.7 x1= 524.4 sz=[10.9] ['URWPalladioL-Roma'] | For RL training across diverse scaffolds, we decouple agent rollout execution into an agent
x=  70.9 y= 737.2 x1= 524.6 sz=[10.9] ['URWPalladioL-Roma'] | sandbox and a worker container. The sandbox runs the scaffold and its tools, while the worker
x= 292.2 y= 778.9 x1= 303.1 sz=[10.9] ['URWPalladioL-Roma'] | 26

x=  70.5 y= 425.6 x1= 524.4 sz=[10.8] ['URWPalladioL-Roma'] | provides a scaffold-agnostic control layer that orchestrates the rollout, normalizes heterogeneous
x=  70.9 y= 439.1 x1= 524.4 sz=[10.9] ['URWPalladioL-Roma'] | interactions into a common trajectory schema, and communicates with the trainer. Both run on
x=  70.9 y= 452.7 x1= 524.4 sz=[10.9] ['URWPalladioL-Roma'] | DSec (Section 5.1.3), outside the preemptible GPU training pool, separating long-lived rollouts
x=  70.9 y= 466.1 x1= 524.4 sz=[11.0] ['URWPalladioL-Roma'] | from fine-grained training scheduling. During trainer preemption, rollout execution can be
x=  70.9 y= 479.8 x1= 524.4 sz=[10.9] ['URWPalladioL-Roma'] | suspended and offloaded while preserving its full state for later resumption and releasing CPU
x=  70.9 y= 493.3 x1= 524.4 sz=[10.9] ['URWPalladioL-Roma'] | and GPU resources. This design enables stable and efficient RL across heterogeneous scaffolds
x=  70.4 y= 506.8 x1= 294.4 sz=[10.9] ['URWPalladioL-Roma'] | without modifying the underlying algorithms.
x=  87.8 y= 527.1 x1= 524.4 sz=[11.0] ['URWPalladioL-Roma'] | To extend effective RL compute beyond a single training run, we use model merging to
x=  70.9 y= 540.6 x1= 524.4 sz=[11.0] ['URWPalladioL-Roma'] | reinitialize successive RL runs. Specifically, we merge checkpoints from runs across different
x=  70.9 y= 554.2 x1= 524.4 sz=[11.0] ['URWPalladioL-Roma'] | scaffolds or configurations, combining improvements acquired along different optimization
x=  70.5 y= 567.7 x1= 524.6 sz=[11.0] ['URWPalladioL-Roma'] | paths. In Figure 7 and Figure 8, disconnected curve segments reflect successive RL runs after
x=  70.9 y= 581.3 x1= 525.8 sz=[10.9] ['URWPalladioL-Roma'] | model reinitialization. This yields further gains in both task performance and token efficiency,
x=  70.5 y= 594.8 x1= 524.4 sz=[11.0] ['URWPalladioL-Roma'] | providing a simple and practical way to aggregate parallel RL compute and continue scaling
x=  70.9 y= 608.5 x1= 180.4 sz=[10.9] ['URWPalladioL-Roma'] | across successive runs.

x=  70.9 y= 248.6 x1= 524.4 sz=[10.8, 10.9] ['URWPalladioL-Roma', 'txsys'] | Figure 8 | Performance improves with cumulative RL steps when jointly training across multiple
x=  70.6 y= 263.4 x1= 524.4 sz=[10.8] ['URWPalladioL-Roma'] | versions of Claude Code (left) and across heterogeneous scaffolds, including OpenCode, Pi, and
x=  70.9 y= 276.9 x1= 524.4 sz=[10.9] ['URWPalladioL-Roma'] | DeepSeek Harness in Standard and PTC modes (right). Performance is evaluated on DeepSWE
x=  70.6 y= 290.4 x1= 476.9 sz=[10.9] ['URWPalladioL-Roma'] | v1.1. Lighter curves show the evaluation of individual scaffold versions or scaffolds.
x=  70.9 y= 325.3 x1= 524.4 sz=[10.9] ['URWPalladioL-Roma'] | ning diverse harnesses, platforms, code repositories, software dependencies, and task-specific
x=  70.9 y= 338.8 x1= 526.2 sz=[11.0] ['URWPalladioL-Roma'] | services. At this scale, the primary bottlenecks shifted toward datacenter scalability, work-
x=  70.9 y= 352.4 x1= 524.8 sz=[10.9] ['URWPalladioL-Roma'] | load isolation, per-node compute density, and the containment of misbehavior by increasingly
x=  70.9 y= 366.0 x1= 385.9 sz=[10.9] ['URWPalladioL-Roma'] | capable agents. We briefly describe our key design aspects below.
x=  87.8 y= 386.1 x1= 526.2 sz=[11.0] ['URWPalladioL-Bold', 'URWPalladioL-Roma'] | Horizontally Scaling Compute at Scale. DSec scales through two complementary mech-
x=  70.9 y= 399.8 x1= 524.6 sz=[11.0] ['URWPalladioL-Roma'] | anisms: sharding and scheduling with relaxed consistency. To accommodate a large number
x=  70.9 y= 413.3 x1= 524.4 sz=[11.0] ['URWPalladioL-Roma'] | of machines, we partition compute nodes into multiple shards (so-called scale units). Such
x=  70.9 y= 427.0 x1= 525.8 sz=[10.8] ['URWPalladioL-Roma'] | sharding also allows us to reduce blast radius by isolating workloads from different experiments,
x=  70.5 y= 440.6 x1= 526.3 sz=[10.8] ['URWPalladioL-Roma'] | preventing a single memory-intensive task from exhausting resources shared by unrelated work.
x=  87.8 y= 460.8 x1= 526.2 sz=[11.0] ['URWPalladioL-Roma'] | Instead of using off-the-shelf orchestrators like Kubernetes, DSec employs a custom place-
x=  70.9 y= 474.3 x1= 524.8 sz=[11.0] ['URWPalladioL-Roma'] | ment engine to schedule the large number of sandboxes, by trading strong global consistency
x=  70.9 y= 487.8 x1= 524.8 sz=[11.0] ['URWPalladioL-Roma'] | for scalability. This design is based on a key observation: agentic sandbox placements only
x=  70.9 y= 501.5 x1= 524.4 sz=[10.8] ['URWPalladioL-Roma'] | demand eventual consistency as long as each compute node enforces local safety constraints. To
x=  70.9 y= 515.0 x1= 524.4 sz=[11.0] ['URWPalladioL-Roma'] | do so, the placement engine deploys in multiple independent replicas, without synchronized
x=  70.9 y= 528.6 x1= 524.4 sz=[10.9] ['URWPalladioL-Roma'] | coordination. Each replica predicts resource availability from recent measurements and makes
x=  70.9 y= 542.0 x1= 524.4 sz=[11.0] ['URWPalladioL-Roma'] | good-enough placement decisions. To compensate for this loss in consistency, each node is
x=  70.9 y= 555.7 x1= 524.4 sz=[10.9] ['URWPalladioL-Roma'] | responsible for validating the final placement decisions, enforcing a hard admission constraint
x=  70.9 y= 569.1 x1= 524.4 sz=[11.0] ['URWPalladioL-Roma'] | that rejects new placements if it exceeds a local warning threshold. This design as a whole
x=  70.9 y= 582.8 x1= 513.7 sz=[10.9] ['URWPalladioL-Roma'] | allows DSec to scale to millions of containers without bottlenecking on central coordination.
x=  87.8 y= 603.0 x1= 526.2 sz=[10.9] ['URWPalladioL-Bold', 'URWPalladioL-Roma'] | Running Sandboxes at High Density. At the node level, we use hardware-supported sub-
x=  70.9 y= 616.6 x1= 524.4 sz=[11.0] ['URWPalladioL-Roma'] | NUMA partitioning and bind each worker VM to an individual NUMA domain. Containers
x=  70.9 y= 630.1 x1= 524.4 sz=[11.0] ['URWPalladioL-Roma'] | run within these worker VMs, with their CPU and memory allocations confined to the VM’s
x=  70.9 y= 643.7 x1= 524.4 sz=[11.0] ['URWPalladioL-Roma'] | local NUMA resources. This setup balances aggressive memory overcommitment against
x=  70.9 y= 657.2 x1= 524.6 sz=[11.0] ['URWPalladioL-Roma'] | Linux kernel lock contention, while localizing memory pressure and runtime failures. Under
x=  70.9 y= 670.8 x1= 524.4 sz=[10.9] ['URWPalladioL-Roma'] | comparable workload configurations, it increases the supported density from roughly 1,000 to
x=  70.9 y= 684.3 x1= 524.4 sz=[11.0] ['URWPalladioL-Roma'] | more than 2,500 concurrent live containers per physical node before measurable end-to-end
x=  70.9 y= 697.9 x1= 173.4 sz=[10.9] ['URWPalladioL-Roma'] | degradation appears.
x=  87.8 y= 718.2 x1= 524.4 sz=[10.9] ['URWPalladioL-Roma'] | Such high-density deployment can nevertheless distort time-sensitive evaluations through
x=  70.9 y= 731.7 x1= 525.5 sz=[11.0] ['URWPalladioL-Roma'] | interference from background workloads. DSec therefore introduces a latency-sensitive (LS)
x=  70.9 y= 744.4 x1= 525.8 sz=[11.0, 12.0] ['LMMono10-Regular', 'URWPalladioL-Roma'] | execution class. We apply SCHED_IDLE to non-LS tasks to minimize their scheduling priority,
x= 292.2 y= 778.9 x1= 303.1 sz=[10.9] ['URWPalladioL-Roma'] | 28

========== PAGE 29 (draw=4) ==========
x=  70.9 y=  88.2 x1= 524.8 sz=[10.9] ['URWPalladioL-Roma'] | and use core scheduling to ensure only tasks of the same priority class execute simultaneously
x=  70.9 y= 101.7 x1= 308.3 sz=[10.9] ['URWPalladioL-Roma'] | on sibling hyperthreads to eliminate interference.
x=  87.8 y= 121.9 x1= 524.4 sz=[11.0] ['URWPalladioL-Bold', 'URWPalladioL-Roma'] | Mitigation of Misbehaving Agents. During RL training, we frequently observe agents
x=  70.9 y= 135.6 x1= 524.4 sz=[10.9] ['URWPalladioL-Roma'] | attempting to perform reward hacking or unintentionally crashing the environment. In certain
x=  70.9 y= 149.1 x1= 524.4 sz=[11.0] ['URWPalladioL-Roma'] | attempts, our agents exploited recently disclosed vulnerabilities, including permission issues
x=  70.9 y= 162.8 x1= 524.6 sz=[10.8] ['URWPalladioL-Roma'] | from the XFS driver, illegal memory access in AppArmor, leaking answers from package mirror
x=  70.9 y= 176.2 x1= 524.4 sz=[11.0] ['URWPalladioL-Roma'] | services, and so on. Agents have also been notorious for deleting critical binaries, breaking
x=  70.9 y= 189.7 x1= 524.4 sz=[11.0] ['URWPalladioL-Roma'] | system files, or even removing the filesystem. We use per-sandbox AppArmor profiles and
x=  70.9 y= 203.3 x1= 524.4 sz=[11.0] ['URWPalladioL-Roma'] | fine-grained eBPF-based network policies to prevent such attempts. If an agent crashes its
x=  70.9 y= 216.8 x1= 524.4 sz=[11.0] ['URWPalladioL-Roma'] | environment, we treat the crash as a failed trajectory and report a "repercussion" signal to the
x=  70.9 y= 230.4 x1= 144.0 sz=[10.9] ['URWPalladioL-Roma'] | RL framework.
x=  70.9 y= 260.6 x1= 276.6 sz=[10.9] ['URWPalladioL-BoldItal'] | 5.1.4. Controllable Reasoning Effort in RL
x=  70.4 y= 283.3 x1= 524.6 sz=[10.8] ['URWPalladioL-Roma'] | Alongside advances in model architecture and hardware, the number of output tokens is another
x=  70.9 y= 296.7 x1= 524.4 sz=[11.0] ['URWPalladioL-Roma'] | key determinant of serving cost and, consequently, of the cost–quality trade-off in real-world
x=  70.9 y= 308.2 x1= 524.4 sz=[10.0, 11.0] ['URWPalladioL-Roma', 'XCharterMathMI'] | applications. We therefore introduce a scalar effort level 𝑏as an explicit conditioning signal
x=  70.9 y= 323.8 x1= 524.4 sz=[11.0] ['URWPalladioL-Roma'] | during reinforcement-learning. This mechanism is applied to both single-turn reasoning and

x=  70.9 y= 389.5 x1= 304.2 sz=[10.9] ['URWPalladioL-Bold'] | 5.2. Asynchronous Post-training Infrastructure
x=  70.5 y= 411.9 x1= 524.6 sz=[11.0] ['URWPalladioL-Roma'] | The long-tail problem during the rollout phase of RL for LLMs has consistently been a major
x=  70.9 y= 425.4 x1= 524.4 sz=[11.0] ['URWPalladioL-Roma'] | bottleneck for training efficiency. To address this, we extend our post-training infrastructure
x=  70.9 y= 439.0 x1= 524.4 sz=[11.0] ['URWPalladioL-Roma'] | to allow asynchronous generation of samples (Zeng et al., 2026; Team et al., 2026b), which
x=  70.9 y= 452.6 x1= 524.4 sz=[10.9] ['URWPalladioL-Roma'] | significantly mitigates the long-tail issue in the rollout phase by maintaining a sufficiently high
x=  70.9 y= 466.1 x1= 524.4 sz=[11.0] ['URWPalladioL-Roma'] | level of concurrency. Asynchronous training is now enabled for nearly all our RL and OPD
x=  70.9 y= 479.7 x1= 338.3 sz=[10.9] ['URWPalladioL-Roma'] | tasks, and rollout efficiency has improved substantially.
x=  70.9 y= 509.7 x1= 189.7 sz=[10.9] ['URWPalladioL-BoldItal'] | 5.2.1. Overall Workflow
x=  70.3 y= 532.2 x1= 525.8 sz=[11.0] ['URWPalladioL-Roma'] | We colocate rollout and training on the same physical devices and time-share their execution,
x=  70.9 y= 545.7 x1= 524.7 sz=[11.0] ['URWPalladioL-Roma'] | eliminating the need to manually tune resource allocation between the two phases. Each task
x=  70.9 y= 559.3 x1= 524.4 sz=[11.0] ['URWPalladioL-Roma'] | specifies an upper bound on the number of in-flight samples, and the system maintains this
x=  70.9 y= 572.9 x1= 247.0 sz=[10.9] ['URWPalladioL-Roma'] | bound throughout the rollout phase.
x=  87.8 y= 593.1 x1= 526.3 sz=[11.0] ['URWPalladioL-Roma'] | We evaluated three dispatch granularities for maintaining the target rollout concurrency.
x=  70.9 y= 606.7 x1= 524.4 sz=[11.0] ['URWPalladioL-Roma'] | Our final approach is sample-level dispatch: once the number of newly completed samples
x=  70.9 y= 620.3 x1= 524.4 sz=[10.9] ['URWPalladioL-Roma'] | reaches the GRPO group size assigned to the next prompt, we dispatch that prompt regardless
x=  70.9 y= 633.9 x1= 524.8 sz=[10.8] ['URWPalladioL-Roma'] | of which groups produced those completions. This helps maintain a steady rollout concurrency
x=  70.9 y= 647.3 x1= 524.4 sz=[11.0] ['URWPalladioL-Roma'] | throughout training. Before settling on this dispatch strategy, we experimented with two
x=  70.9 y= 660.9 x1= 524.4 sz=[11.0] ['URWPalladioL-Roma'] | alternative dispatch granularities. In the first attempt, we dispatched several extra batches at
x=  70.9 y= 674.5 x1= 524.4 sz=[10.9] ['URWPalladioL-Roma'] | the beginning and supplemented a full batch after each training iteration; however, this caused
x=  70.9 y= 688.1 x1= 524.4 sz=[10.9] ['URWPalladioL-Roma'] | severe oscillations in training metrics, indicating that batch-level granularity was too coarse. In
x=  70.9 y= 701.7 x1= 524.4 sz=[10.8] ['URWPalladioL-Roma'] | the second attempt, we switched to prompt-level dispatch, where a new prompt was dispatched
x=  70.9 y= 715.1 x1= 524.4 sz=[11.0] ['URWPalladioL-Roma'] | after one GRPO group finishes, but found that it stalled easily on long-tail samples within a
x=  70.9 y= 728.7 x1= 479.7 sz=[10.9] ['URWPalladioL-Roma'] | GRPO group, making it difficult to smoothly maintain the target rollout concurrency.
x=  87.8 y= 749.1 x1= 526.2 sz=[10.9] ['URWPalladioL-Roma'] | Once enough training samples have accumulated, training preempts ongoing rollouts. Dur-
x= 292.2 y= 778.9 x1= 303.1 sz=[10.9] ['URWPalladioL-Roma'] | 30

========== PAGE 31 (draw=0) ==========
x=  70.9 y=  88.1 x1= 525.8 sz=[11.0] ['URWPalladioL-Roma'] | ing training, we use concatenated routing-replay: for samples that span multiple checkpoints,
x=  70.4 y= 101.8 x1= 524.4 sz=[10.8] ['URWPalladioL-Roma'] | we concatenate the expert routing produced at each rollout segment rather than discarding and
x=  70.9 y= 115.3 x1= 360.9 sz=[10.9] ['URWPalladioL-Roma'] | recomputing the routing information with new checkpoints.
x=  70.9 y= 145.6 x1= 324.6 sz=[10.9] ['URWPalladioL-BoldItal'] | 5.2.2. Mitigating Length Bias and Off-Policy Effects
x=  70.4 y= 168.1 x1= 524.4 sz=[11.0] ['URWPalladioL-Roma'] | Asynchronous generation, while effectively improving rollout efficiency, introduces two side
x=  70.9 y= 181.7 x1= 524.4 sz=[10.9] ['URWPalladioL-Roma'] | effects that can degrade training quality. First, it creates a length-distribution bias, especially in
x=  70.9 y= 195.2 x1= 524.4 sz=[11.0] ['URWPalladioL-Roma'] | the early training stage, because shorter sequences tend to complete first and thus dominate
x=  70.9 y= 208.7 x1= 524.4 sz=[11.0] ['URWPalladioL-Roma'] | the initial training batches. Second, it inevitably produces off-policy samples, i.e., samples
x=  70.4 y= 222.4 x1= 524.4 sz=[10.8] ['URWPalladioL-Roma'] | whose tokens are partially or entirely generated by earlier checkpoints. These two issues require
x=  70.9 y= 235.9 x1= 209.8 sz=[10.9] ['URWPalladioL-Roma'] | different handling strategies.
x=  87.8 y= 256.1 x1= 524.4 sz=[11.0] ['URWPalladioL-Roma'] | To address the length bias, we employ two mechanisms. First, the dispatcher can limit
x=  70.9 y= 269.7 x1= 524.4 sz=[11.0] ['URWPalladioL-Roma'] | concurrency on a per-dataset basis, which helps regulate the proportion of each dataset in the
x=  70.9 y= 283.3 x1= 524.4 sz=[10.9] ['URWPalladioL-Roma'] | steady-state training batch, indirectly mitigating the length skew by controlling the sources of
x=  70.9 y= 296.9 x1= 524.4 sz=[10.9] ['URWPalladioL-Roma'] | incoming samples. Second, we support discarding early-returned short samples to smooth the
x=  70.9 y= 310.4 x1= 524.4 sz=[11.0] ['URWPalladioL-Roma'] | transition into the steady-state length distribution, and prevent the model from overfitting to
x=  70.9 y= 324.0 x1= 183.5 sz=[10.9] ['URWPalladioL-Roma'] | overly short sequences.
x=  87.8 y= 344.4 x1= 524.4 sz=[10.8] ['URWPalladioL-Roma'] | For the off-policy issue, we implement two additional mechanisms. First, by tuning the logic
x=  70.9 y= 357.9 x1= 524.4 sz=[10.9] ['URWPalladioL-Roma'] | that controls sample dispatching and the waiting condition for training samples, we can bound
x=  70.9 y= 371.4 x1= 524.4 sz=[10.8] ['URWPalladioL-Roma'] | the maximum off-policy ratio, ensuring that the training data does not deviate excessively from
x=  70.9 y= 384.9 x1= 524.4 sz=[10.9] ['URWPalladioL-Roma'] | the current model. Second, during training, we add a loss masking scheme that eliminates the
x=  70.9 y= 398.5 x1= 524.4 sz=[10.9] ['URWPalladioL-Roma'] | contribution of tokens with excessive staleness, thereby mitigating the adverse impact of stale
x=  70.9 y= 412.0 x1= 212.8 sz=[10.9] ['URWPalladioL-Roma'] | samples on gradient updates.
x=  70.9 y= 442.3 x1= 232.1 sz=[10.9] ['URWPalladioL-BoldItal'] | 5.2.3. Performance Optimization
x=  70.9 y= 464.8 x1= 524.4 sz=[11.0] ['URWPalladioL-Roma'] | In our asynchronous RL framework, the rollout phase is periodically interrupted to switch to
x=  70.9 y= 478.4 x1= 524.4 sz=[11.0] ['URWPalladioL-Roma'] | updated policy checkpoints. We aim to make this process seamless: interruptions should be
x=  70.9 y= 492.0 x1= 456.0 sz=[10.9] ['URWPalladioL-Roma'] | near-instantaneous, and interrupted rollouts should resume as if never stopped.
x=  87.8 y= 512.3 x1= 524.4 sz=[10.9] ['URWPalladioL-Roma'] | To stop rollouts promptly, we support token-level interruption: generation can be halted at
x=  70.9 y= 525.9 x1= 524.7 sz=[10.9] ['URWPalladioL-Roma'] | any token boundary. Once sufficient training data has been collected, all in-flight samples stop
x=  70.9 y= 539.4 x1= 469.4 sz=[10.9] ['URWPalladioL-Roma'] | almost immediately, allowing the system to enter the training phase without delay.
x=  87.8 y= 559.8 x1= 524.4 sz=[10.8] ['URWPalladioL-Roma'] | To preserve rollout progress across checkpoint switches, rollout states such as KV cache and
x=  70.9 y= 573.2 x1= 524.4 sz=[11.0] ['URWPalladioL-Roma'] | expert routing are persisted at token granularity during generation. Upon resumption with a
x=  70.9 y= 586.9 x1= 524.4 sz=[10.8] ['URWPalladioL-Roma'] | new checkpoint, the persisted states are directly reused, eliminating the cost of re-prefilling and
x=  70.9 y= 600.5 x1= 524.4 sz=[10.8] ['URWPalladioL-Roma'] | allowing interrupted samples to continue exactly where they left off. Since this requires retaining
x=  70.9 y= 614.0 x1= 524.4 sz=[10.8] ['URWPalladioL-Roma'] | the states of all in-flight samples, we perform sample-grained garbage collection, releasing each
x=  70.9 y= 627.5 x1= 257.9 sz=[10.9] ['URWPalladioL-Roma'] | sample’s states as soon as it completes.
x=  87.8 y= 647.8 x1= 526.2 sz=[10.9] ['URWPalladioL-Roma'] | Beyond checkpoint switching, the same fast-interruption and seamless-resumption machin-
x=  70.9 y= 661.3 x1= 524.4 sz=[11.0] ['URWPalladioL-Roma'] | ery allows the training jobs to respond promptly to cluster scheduling preemption signals
x=  70.4 y= 674.9 x1= 407.0 sz=[10.9] ['URWPalladioL-Roma'] | without losing progress, thereby improving overall cluster utilization.
x=  70.9 y= 705.2 x1= 272.2 sz=[10.9] ['URWPalladioL-BoldItal'] | 5.2.4. Large-Scale On-Policy Distillation
x=  70.4 y= 727.8 x1= 524.4 sz=[10.9] ['URWPalladioL-Roma'] | As the last stage of post-training, the final full-vocabulary OPD task is trained on datasets from
x=  70.9 y= 741.3 x1= 524.4 sz=[10.9] ['URWPalladioL-Roma'] | all domains using over 40 teacher models. It also adopts asynchronous generation to improve
x= 292.2 y= 778.9 x1= 303.1 sz=[10.9] ['URWPalladioL-Roma'] | 31

========== PAGE 32 (draw=0) ==========
x=  70.9 y=  88.2 x1= 524.6 sz=[10.9] ['URWPalladioL-Roma'] | rollout efficiency. Due to differences in training procedures across domains, the best teacher for
x=  70.9 y= 101.6 x1= 524.6 sz=[11.0] ['URWPalladioL-Roma'] | each domain may come from a different stage of model development. Moreover, the teacher
x=  70.9 y= 115.2 x1= 524.4 sz=[11.0] ['URWPalladioL-Roma'] | models may differ architecturally from one another and from the student. Our post-training
x=  70.9 y= 128.7 x1= 524.4 sz=[11.0] ['URWPalladioL-Roma'] | infrastructure readily accommodates this setting, supporting full-vocabulary OPD with an
x=  70.9 y= 142.5 x1= 524.4 sz=[10.8] ['URWPalladioL-Roma'] | effectively unbounded number of architecturally heterogeneous teachers, and efficient switching
x=  70.9 y= 155.9 x1= 325.2 sz=[10.9] ['URWPalladioL-Roma'] | among them at negligible cost (DeepSeek-AI, 2026b).
x=  87.8 y= 176.2 x1= 524.8 sz=[11.0] ['URWPalladioL-Roma'] | The OPD stage also requires dynamic reconfiguration during training. We continuously
x=  70.9 y= 189.7 x1= 524.4 sz=[11.0] ['URWPalladioL-Roma'] | track model capabilities and may adjust the training recipe accordingly, including the dataset
x=  70.9 y= 203.4 x1= 524.4 sz=[10.9] ['URWPalladioL-Roma'] | mixture, per-dataset concurrency limits, and active teachers. Such changes are straightforward
x=  70.9 y= 216.9 x1= 524.4 sz=[10.8] ['URWPalladioL-Roma'] | in synchronous training where rollout batches provide explicit configuration boundaries. In the
x=  70.9 y= 230.4 x1= 524.4 sz=[10.9] ['URWPalladioL-Roma'] | asynchronous setting, however, samples generated under different configurations may coexist
x=  70.9 y= 243.9 x1= 524.4 sz=[11.0] ['URWPalladioL-Roma'] | in flight. Our infrastructure supports consistent transitions between configurations without
x=  70.9 y= 257.5 x1= 213.9 sz=[10.9] ['URWPalladioL-Roma'] | disrupting rollout or training.

x=  70.9 y= 142.4 x1= 526.1 sz=[10.8] ['URWPalladioL-Roma'] | measurable gap still remains when benchmarked against the leading closed-source alternatives.
x=  87.8 y= 162.6 x1= 524.4 sz=[11.0] ['URWPalladioL-Roma'] | Beyond peak performance, DeepSeek-V4.1-Flash exposes a reasoning-effort setting that
x=  70.9 y= 176.2 x1= 524.4 sz=[11.0] ['URWPalladioL-Roma'] | allows users to trade inference cost for accuracy in a controllable manner. As illustrated in
x=  70.9 y= 189.9 x1= 524.4 sz=[10.8] ['URWPalladioL-Roma'] | Figure 9, both accuracy and output length increase steadily with the effort level across reasoning
x=  70.9 y= 203.4 x1= 524.4 sz=[10.8] ['URWPalladioL-Roma'] | and agentic benchmarks alike. Raising the effort from 25 to 100 improves the average Pass@1 on
x=  70.9 y= 216.8 x1= 524.4 sz=[11.0] ['URWPalladioL-Roma'] | eight reasoning-intensive benchmarks from 67.1% to 76.3%, on DeepSWE v1.1 from 66.0% to
x=  70.9 y= 229.3 x1= 524.4 sz=[10.9] ['URWPalladioL-Roma', 'txsys'] | 74.2%, and on Terminal-Bench 2.1 from 82.4% to 90.6%, at the cost of roughly 2.5× more output
x=  70.9 y= 243.9 x1= 524.8 sz=[11.0] ['URWPalladioL-Roma'] | tokens. Notably, the effort control learned on single-response reasoning transfers faithfully
x=  70.9 y= 257.5 x1= 524.4 sz=[11.0] ['URWPalladioL-Roma'] | to long-horizon agentic trajectories, where it governs the total amount of exploration and
x=  70.6 y= 271.0 x1= 524.4 sz=[11.0] ['URWPalladioL-Roma'] | verification across turns. The gains are front-loaded: the 60–80 range already recovers most
x=  70.9 y= 284.6 x1= 524.4 sz=[11.0] ['URWPalladioL-Roma'] | of the accuracy of the maximum setting at less than half of its token budget, whereas the final
x=  70.9 y= 297.0 x1= 524.4 sz=[10.9] ['URWPalladioL-Roma', 'txsys'] | step to effort 100 lengthens agent trajectories by 1.6–1.8× for only marginal improvements. The
x=  70.9 y= 311.8 x1= 524.4 sz=[10.9] ['URWPalladioL-Roma'] | maximum tier is thus best reserved for the most challenging tasks, while moderate effort levels
x=  70.9 y= 325.3 x1= 401.5 sz=[10.9] ['URWPalladioL-Roma'] | offer a favorable cost–performance balance for everyday agentic use.
x=  70.9 y= 355.4 x1= 279.2 sz=[10.9] ['URWPalladioL-BoldItal'] | 5.3.3. Performance across reasoning efforts
x=  70.9 y= 378.0 x1= 524.4 sz=[10.9] ['URWPalladioL-Roma'] | Figure 9 reports performance and average response length across a range of budget values. As
x=  70.9 y= 391.4 x1= 524.4 sz=[11.0] ['URWPalladioL-Roma'] | the reasoning effort increases, the model produces progressively longer reasoning traces and
x=  70.9 y= 405.1 x1= 524.4 sz=[10.8] ['URWPalladioL-Roma'] | accuracy improves monotonically on reasoning-intensive benchmarks and software engineering
x=  70.9 y= 418.5 x1= 524.4 sz=[11.0] ['URWPalladioL-Roma'] | tasks, with the largest gains concentrated in the low-to-mid budget range and diminishing
x=  70.9 y= 432.1 x1= 524.4 sz=[11.0] ['URWPalladioL-Roma'] | returns beyond. Although our RL involves only a limited number of effort levels, the use of
x=  70.9 y= 445.6 x1= 526.3 sz=[11.0] ['URWPalladioL-Roma'] | scalar efforts achieves flexible, interpolated control of response length within a specific range.
x=  70.5 y= 459.2 x1= 524.4 sz=[11.0] ['URWPalladioL-Roma'] | This allows practitioners to trade off quality against latency and token cost along a smooth
x=  70.9 y= 472.7 x1= 524.8 sz=[11.0] ['URWPalladioL-Roma'] | continuum: latency-sensitive applications can operate at low effort with modest accuracy
x=  70.9 y= 486.3 x1= 524.4 sz=[11.0] ['URWPalladioL-Roma'] | degradation, while difficult tasks can invoke high effort to recover the model’s full reasoning
x=  70.9 y= 499.8 x1= 524.4 sz=[11.0] ['URWPalladioL-Roma'] | capability. In our public API service, we expose three preset effort levels that map onto this
x=  70.9 y= 512.5 x1= 478.0 sz=[10.9, 12.0] ['LMMono10-Regular', 'URWPalladioL-Roma'] | scale: low, high, and max correspond to effort values of 50, 75, and 100, respectively.
x=  70.9 y= 543.5 x1= 270.9 sz=[10.9] ['URWPalladioL-BoldItal'] | 5.3.4. Performance across agent scaffolds

x=  70.9 y= 701.6 x1= 193.4 sz=[10.9] ['URWPalladioL-Roma'] | and Terminal-Bench v2.1.

x=  70.9 y= 538.5 x1= 161.5 sz=[10.9] ['URWPalladioL-BoldItal'] | 5.3.5. Multi-Agent
x=  70.5 y= 561.0 x1= 524.4 sz=[11.0] ['URWPalladioL-Roma'] | To explore multi-agent collaboration on complex tasks, we conduct preliminary experiments
x=  70.4 y= 574.7 x1= 421.5 sz=[10.9] ['URWPalladioL-Roma'] | with DeepSeek-V4.1-Flash using DeepSeek Harness’s Agent Team mode.
x=  87.8 y= 595.0 x1= 524.4 sz=[10.8] ['URWPalladioL-Bold', 'URWPalladioL-Roma'] | Multi-Agent Harnesses. We use DeepSeek Harness in Agent Team mode, where a lead agent
x=  70.9 y= 607.6 x1= 526.3 sz=[10.9, 12.0] ['LMMono10-Regular', 'URWPalladioL-Roma'] | can asynchronously create named, persistent teammates by default through spawn_teammate.
x=  70.9 y= 621.1 x1= 524.7 sz=[11.0, 12.0] ['LMMono10-Regular', 'URWPalladioL-Roma'] | Each receives a delegated task and starts in either fresh mode without lead history or fork
x=  70.9 y= 635.6 x1= 524.8 sz=[10.9] ['URWPalladioL-Roma'] | mode with a one-time snapshot of the lead’s completed turns. All agents share one repository
x=  70.9 y= 649.2 x1= 354.8 sz=[10.9] ['URWPalladioL-Roma'] | checkout, making edits immediately visible to one another.
x=  87.8 y= 668.6 x1= 532.8 sz=[10.8, 12.0] ['LMMono10-Regular', 'URWPalladioL-Roma'] | Agents communicate through a durable peer mailbox. A message sent through send_message
x=  70.9 y= 683.0 x1= 525.8 sz=[11.0] ['URWPalladioL-Roma'] | reaches a running teammate at its next step boundary, starts a new turn for an idle teammate,
x=  70.9 y= 696.5 x1= 524.4 sz=[11.0] ['URWPalladioL-Roma'] | or resumes an inactive teammate. Across each teammate’s task lifecycle, the lead monitors
x=  70.9 y= 709.2 x1= 524.4 sz=[11.0, 12.0] ['LMMono10-Regular', 'URWPalladioL-Roma'] | runtime status with list_agents and waits for status, mailbox, or shared-task changes with
x=  70.6 y= 722.8 x1= 524.4 sz=[11.0, 12.0] ['LMMono10-Regular', 'URWPalladioL-Roma'] | wait_agent. Task ownership, dependencies, and advisory write scopes are maintained on
x=  70.9 y= 736.3 x1= 526.3 sz=[11.0, 12.0] ['LMMono10-Regular', 'URWPalladioL-Roma'] | a shared task board (using the four team_task_* tools with revision checks on updates).
