x=  70.9 y= 248.6 x1= 526.2 sz=[10.9, 11.0] ['URWPalladioL-Roma', 'txsys'] | Figure 10 | Test-time compute scaling for single-agent and multi-agent configurations on Pro-
x=  70.9 y= 263.4 x1= 524.7 sz=[10.8] ['URWPalladioL-Roma'] | gramBench (Almost@1) and FrontierSWE v2 (Mean@5) as functions of the per-rollout wall-clock
x=  70.9 y= 276.8 x1= 115.5 sz=[10.9] ['URWPalladioL-Roma'] | deadline.
x=  70.3 y= 311.7 x1= 524.4 sz=[11.0] ['URWPalladioL-Roma'] | When intervention is needed, only the lead can interrupt a teammate’s current turn through
x=  70.9 y= 324.4 x1= 526.2 sz=[11.0, 12.0] ['LMMono10-Regular', 'URWPalladioL-Roma'] | interrupt_agent. Once the required work is complete, the lead reviews and tests the com-
x=  70.9 y= 338.9 x1= 298.7 sz=[10.9] ['URWPalladioL-Roma'] | bined changes and produces the final response.
x=  87.8 y= 359.0 x1= 524.4 sz=[11.0] ['URWPalladioL-Bold', 'URWPalladioL-Roma'] | Training. We train Agent Team mode with an RL reward combining task performance, a
x=  70.9 y= 372.8 x1= 526.2 sz=[10.9] ['URWPalladioL-Roma'] | collaboration bonus that encourages delegation and inter-agent communication, and a derived-
x=  70.9 y= 386.3 x1= 526.2 sz=[10.8] ['URWPalladioL-Roma'] | latency penalty that promotes efficient coordination. Derived latency is computed by represent-
x=  70.9 y= 399.8 x1= 525.8 sz=[11.0] ['URWPalladioL-Roma'] | ing execution events and their collaboration dependencies as a directed acyclic graph (DAG),
x=  70.9 y= 413.3 x1= 524.4 sz=[11.0] ['URWPalladioL-Roma'] | assigning costs from token counts at fixed prefill/decode rates plus measured tool-execution
x=  70.9 y= 427.0 x1= 526.2 sz=[10.8] ['URWPalladioL-Roma'] | time, and taking the length of the critical path. This encourages useful parallelism while penaliz-
x=  70.9 y= 440.5 x1= 524.4 sz=[10.9] ['URWPalladioL-Roma'] | ing unnecessary sequential work and synchronization, with reduced sensitivity to serving-side
x=  70.9 y= 454.0 x1= 213.4 sz=[10.9] ['URWPalladioL-Roma'] | batching and queuing delays.
x=  87.8 y= 474.2 x1= 525.5 sz=[11.0] ['URWPalladioL-Bold', 'URWPalladioL-Roma'] | Performance. We construct a high-confidence subset of ProgramBench (Yang et al., 2026)
x=  70.9 y= 487.9 x1= 524.4 sz=[11.0] ['URWPalladioL-Roma'] | by retaining only tasks for which the reference solution achieves a pass rate of at least 95% on
x=  70.9 y= 501.4 x1= 524.4 sz=[11.0] ['URWPalladioL-Roma'] | the hidden test suite. This filtering procedure leaves 172 "golden" tasks. We also evaluate on
x=  70.9 y= 515.0 x1= 524.4 sz=[10.9] ['URWPalladioL-Roma'] | FrontierSWE v2 (Kondra et al., 2026), a larger and more challenging successor to FrontierSWE
x=  70.9 y= 528.5 x1= 524.4 sz=[11.0] ['URWPalladioL-Roma'] | that uses a substantially improved methodology. We construct a no-GPU subset from the
x=  70.9 y= 542.2 x1= 524.4 sz=[10.8] ['URWPalladioL-Roma'] | currently public tasks by excluding tasks that require GPU access. We evaluate single-agent and
x=  70.9 y= 555.7 x1= 526.3 sz=[10.9] ['URWPalladioL-Roma'] | multi-agent configurations on both benchmarks under explicit per-rollout wall-clock deadlines.
x=  70.5 y= 569.1 x1= 524.4 sz=[11.0] ['URWPalladioL-Roma'] | The results reported here are preliminary: we compare the strongest observed multi-agent
x=  70.9 y= 582.7 x1= 524.4 sz=[11.0] ['URWPalladioL-Roma'] | configurations with the strongest available single-agent baselines. On ProgramBench, we run
x=  70.9 y= 596.3 x1= 524.4 sz=[10.9] ['URWPalladioL-Roma'] | up to three rollouts per task, corresponding to 516 planned rollouts for each configuration. We
x=  70.9 y= 609.9 x1= 524.4 sz=[10.8] ['URWPalladioL-Roma'] | report Almost@1, which measures the fraction of individual rollouts achieving a score of at least
x=  70.9 y= 623.5 x1= 525.8 sz=[10.8] ['URWPalladioL-Roma'] | 0.95. On FrontierSWE v2, we report Mean@5. ProgramBench deadlines range from 1 to 12 hours,
x=  70.4 y= 636.9 x1= 525.8 sz=[11.0] ['URWPalladioL-Roma'] | while FrontierSWE v2 is evaluated at deadlines ranging from 1 to 20 hours. At each deadline,
x=  70.9 y= 650.5 x1= 451.7 sz=[10.9] ['URWPalladioL-Roma'] | metrics are computed from the outputs available when the deadline is reached.
x=  87.8 y= 670.8 x1= 526.2 sz=[11.0] ['URWPalladioL-Roma'] | As shown in Figure 10, multi-agent configurations outperform their single-agent counter-
x=  70.5 y= 684.3 x1= 524.4 sz=[11.0] ['URWPalladioL-Roma'] | parts at every deadline on both benchmarks. On ProgramBench, Almost@1 increases from
x=  70.3 y= 698.0 x1= 524.4 sz=[10.8] ['URWPalladioL-Roma'] | 13.59% at 1 hour to a peak of 30.04% at 8 hours for the multi-agent configuration, compared with
x=  70.3 y= 711.4 x1= 524.4 sz=[11.0] ['URWPalladioL-Roma'] | 12.79% and 20.39% for the single-agent configuration. On FrontierSWE v2, Mean@5 increases
x=  70.9 y= 725.0 x1= 524.4 sz=[10.9] ['URWPalladioL-Roma'] | from 13.50% at 1 hour to 32.90% at 20 hours for the multi-agent configuration, compared with
x=  70.9 y= 738.6 x1= 402.3 sz=[10.9] ['URWPalladioL-Roma'] | an increase from 10.50% to 28.20% for the single-agent configuration.

x=  70.9 y=  86.6 x1= 365.4 sz=[13.0] ['URWPalladioL-Bold'] | 6. Conclusion, Limitations, and Future Directions
x=  70.9 y= 113.6 x1= 524.4 sz=[10.8] ['URWPalladioL-Roma'] | In this work, we introduce DeepSeek-V4.1-Flash, a multimodal Mixture-of-Experts (MoE) model
x=  70.4 y= 127.0 x1= 524.4 sz=[11.0] ['URWPalladioL-Roma'] | with support for contexts of up to one million tokens. Through joint optimization of model
x=  70.9 y= 140.6 x1= 524.4 sz=[10.9] ['URWPalladioL-Roma'] | architecture, cache precision, and deployment strategy, DeepSeek-V4.1-Flash pushes the limits
x=  70.9 y= 154.0 x1= 524.4 sz=[11.0] ['URWPalladioL-Roma'] | of KV cache compression. Its Causal Encoder-Decoder (CED) architecture enables the model
x=  70.9 y= 167.6 x1= 525.8 sz=[11.0] ['URWPalladioL-Roma'] | to activate only 8B parameters per token during prefill, compared with 16B during decode,
x=  70.9 y= 181.2 x1= 526.2 sz=[10.9] ['URWPalladioL-Roma'] | improving cost efficiency for input-heavy agentic workloads. At equal sequence lengths, cross-
x=  70.9 y= 194.7 x1= 524.4 sz=[11.0] ['URWPalladioL-Roma'] | layer KV cache reuse in Compressed Sparse Attention 2 (CSA2) and FP4 KV caching reduce
x=  70.9 y= 208.2 x1= 524.4 sz=[11.0] ['URWPalladioL-Roma'] | its global KV cache footprint (always in HBM) to 890 bytes per token, roughly 1/4 of the
x=  70.9 y= 221.8 x1= 524.4 sz=[11.0] ['URWPalladioL-Roma'] | corresponding footprint of DeepSeek-V4-Flash. SWA Bounded Replay further reduces its
x=  70.5 y= 235.3 x1= 524.4 sz=[11.0] ['URWPalladioL-Roma'] | persistent KV cache footprint (always on SSD or in host memory) to roughly 1/8 of that of
x=  70.9 y= 248.9 x1= 524.4 sz=[11.0] ['URWPalladioL-Roma'] | DeepSeek-V4-Flash. These reductions alleviate HBM and SSD capacity pressure while the
x=  70.9 y= 262.4 x1= 524.4 sz=[11.0] ['URWPalladioL-Roma'] | model delivers substantially better overall performance than DeepSeek-V4-Flash. Despite
x=  70.5 y= 276.1 x1= 524.4 sz=[10.9] ['URWPalladioL-Roma'] | possessing a significantly smaller parameter footprint than contemporary open-source models
x=  70.9 y= 289.5 x1= 525.8 sz=[11.0] ['URWPalladioL-Roma'] | such as GLM-5.3 and Kimi-K3, DeepSeek-V4.1 achieves comparable—and in several tasks,
x=  70.9 y= 303.2 x1= 300.8 sz=[10.9] ['URWPalladioL-Roma'] | superior—performance across key benchmarks.
x=  87.8 y= 323.4 x1= 524.4 sz=[11.0] ['URWPalladioL-Roma'] | Although DeepSeek-V4.1-Flash substantially simplifies several architectural components
x=  70.9 y= 337.1 x1= 524.4 sz=[10.8] ['URWPalladioL-Roma'] | relative to DeepSeek-V4-Flash, the newly introduced architectural changes also create robustness
x=  70.9 y= 350.5 x1= 524.4 sz=[11.0] ['URWPalladioL-Roma'] | boundaries that have yet to be fully characterized. Our internal evaluations cover a diverse
x=  70.9 y= 364.1 x1= 526.2 sz=[11.0] ['URWPalladioL-Roma'] | range of test cases and boundary conditions, and we have not observed any systematic degra-
x=  70.9 y= 377.6 x1= 524.4 sz=[11.0] ['URWPalladioL-Roma'] | dation in model capabilities in the evaluated settings. Nevertheless, no finite test suite can
x=  70.9 y= 391.2 x1= 524.4 sz=[11.0] ['URWPalladioL-Roma'] | cover every extreme input and deployment condition. Potential selection errors in CSA2 and
x=  70.9 y= 404.9 x1= 524.4 sz=[10.8] ['URWPalladioL-Roma'] | approximate state reconstruction in SWA Bounded Replay may still cause capability degradation
x=  70.9 y= 418.3 x1= 524.4 sz=[10.9] ['URWPalladioL-Roma'] | in untested boundary cases. Going forward, we will continue to expand our stress-testing and
x=  70.9 y= 431.9 x1= 524.4 sz=[10.9] ['URWPalladioL-Roma'] | evaluation stack, with particular attention to sparse retrieval over long contexts and SWA state
x=  70.9 y= 445.4 x1= 525.8 sz=[11.0] ['URWPalladioL-Roma'] | reconstruction at cache-resumption boundaries. We will also monitor real-world workloads,
x=  70.9 y= 458.9 x1= 524.6 sz=[11.0] ['URWPalladioL-Roma'] | systematically characterize potential failure modes and robustness boundaries, and further
x=  70.9 y= 472.5 x1= 328.8 sz=[10.9] ['URWPalladioL-Roma'] | improve model robustness under extreme conditions.
x=  87.8 y= 492.9 x1= 524.4 sz=[10.8] ['URWPalladioL-Roma'] | As AI models achieve remarkable performance capabilities, standard evaluation benchmarks
x=  70.9 y= 506.4 x1= 524.4 sz=[11.0] ['URWPalladioL-Roma'] | have increasingly reached saturation. While DeepSeek-V4.1-Flash demonstrates performance
x=  70.9 y= 520.0 x1= 526.2 sz=[10.8] ['URWPalladioL-Roma'] | that closely approaches top-tier models like Fable-5 and GPT-6 Astra—offering a highly compa-
x=  70.9 y= 533.6 x1= 524.4 sz=[10.8] ['URWPalladioL-Roma'] | rable user experience in daily applications—a performance gap remains on the most challenging
x=  70.9 y= 547.0 x1= 524.4 sz=[11.0] ['URWPalladioL-Roma'] | tasks. Although benchmark scores show a narrow margin, this parity does not imply that the
x=  70.9 y= 560.5 x1= 526.2 sz=[11.0] ['URWPalladioL-Roma'] | model matches the frontier capabilities of leading closed-source systems on complex, high-
x=  70.9 y= 574.2 x1= 242.3 sz=[10.9] ['URWPalladioL-Roma'] | difficulty reasoning and edge cases.
x=  87.8 y= 594.4 x1= 524.4 sz=[11.0] ['URWPalladioL-Roma'] | Consequently, we will continuously update our evaluation protocols to ensure rigorous
x=  70.9 y= 607.9 x1= 524.4 sz=[11.0] ['URWPalladioL-Roma'] | assessment of state-of-the-art reasoning boundaries. Alongside continued efforts to reduce
x=  70.9 y= 621.5 x1= 526.2 sz=[11.0] ['URWPalladioL-Roma'] | model costs, we believe that further advances in model intelligence will depend on the coor-
x=  70.9 y= 635.1 x1= 524.4 sz=[11.0] ['URWPalladioL-Roma'] | dinated scaling of data, model capacity, and RL. With DeepSeek-V4.1-Flash as a new starting
x=  70.5 y= 648.6 x1= 524.4 sz=[11.0] ['URWPalladioL-Roma'] | point, we will continue to explore the limits of model capabilities and systematically address
x=  70.9 y= 662.1 x1= 524.4 sz=[11.0] ['URWPalladioL-Roma'] | key challenges in large-scale data synthesis and RL scaling. We will also actively integrate
x=  70.9 y= 675.8 x1= 526.2 sz=[10.8] ['URWPalladioL-Roma'] | model–harness co-design, enabling the joint system to evolve and be optimized together. By ad-
x=  70.6 y= 689.4 x1= 524.4 sz=[10.8] ['URWPalladioL-Roma'] | vancing cost reduction and capability scaling in tandem, we hope to make highly capable agents
x=  70.9 y= 702.9 x1= 524.4 sz=[10.9] ['URWPalladioL-Roma'] | more accessible and easier to deploy, further lowering the barriers to adopting AI technologies
x=  70.9 y= 716.4 x1= 313.8 sz=[10.9] ['URWPalladioL-Roma'] | across a broader range of industries and scenarios.
x= 292.2 y= 778.9 x1= 303.1 sz=[10.9] ['URWPalladioL-Roma'] | 37
